+# Final harness (branch `final-harness`)
+
+This branch is `main` with `start-pose-live-guard` and `hybrid-harness` merged in, the LiveKit observer from
+`codex/hill-climb-livekit`, and two fixes the merge needed. This file records what was kept, what was left out and
+why, and how to run a match. The how-to for every flag is in [HARNESS_GUIDE.md](HARNESS_GUIDE.md).
+
+## Run a match
+
+```bash
+python -m harness plans/hill_climb_v4.yaml --live --match --detect plans/table_colors.yaml --live-guard
+```
+
+`--match` refuses to start (exit 2) with `--budget`, a `manual` / `+manual` verifier, or a stage a person would
+answer. It turns the operator console off, reads practice lessons without writing new ones, and records
+`match: true` in the manifest. It warns if `--detect ... --live-guard` is missing. Without the live guard, nothing
+stops an 8-ball between monitor verdicts.
+
+For base Dream-1 (no fine-tune), use `plans/hybrid_v4.yaml` instead. It has specific colour and position prompts,
+monitor-written prompt rewrites, `reset_first` and the "no power" prefix. Drop its cable 3 and 4 stages, which
+cost about 18 points a match in the sim:
+
+```bash
+python -m harness plans/hybrid_v4.yaml --live --match --skip cable_extra --detect plans/table_colors.yaml --live-guard
+```
+
+For the LiveKit points, show the referee `python -m harness.livekit_observer` before the match (guide §6h). It only
+subscribes and is never part of the run.
+
+## What's in, and where it came from
+
+| Piece | From | Why it's in |
+|---|---|---|
+| Runner state machine, `confirm: 2`, monitor verifier, `guard:` with `max_blind_s` | main | The core. A false "done" is a DQ, so every gated stage needs two agreeing verdicts. |
+| Local colour detector `--detect` | main | Local, so it answers in milliseconds where the monitor takes seconds. It feeds both guards and draws boxes on the monitor's frames. |
+| Live guard `--live-guard` (10 Hz on `/frame/<wrist>.jpg`, 3 frames in a row, sends `/stop` itself) | start-pose-live-guard | The only thing fast enough to stop an 8-ball. See the stress results below. |
+| Start-pose gate (`start_pose:` + `plans/start_poses.json`) | start-pose-live-guard | It won't send a prompt from a pose the policy never started from. |
+| `decide.py` | start-pose-live-guard | Kept in shadow mode only: it logs what it would do and decides nothing. |
+| Stitched history grid + `parallel: 2` (now in `hill_climb_v4.yaml` too) | hybrid-harness | Measured on the station: a verdict every ~4.9 s instead of ~9.7 s. Judge speed is the main lever in the sim. |
+| Light frames (`/frame/<cam>.jpg?w=640`, ~35 KB) | hybrid-harness | Dream's round trip went from 2–6 s to ~0.8 s once the harness stopped pulling ~2 MB debug-view trips. |
+| Gripper sensors (`GET /arms`) overrule the monitor's "holding" | hybrid-harness | A gripper closed on nothing is not holding a cable, whatever the image seems to show. |
+| `rewrite_prompts` (monitor `next_prompt`, 15 s cooldown, max 3 per attempt) | hybrid-harness | It gets base Dream-1 off the wrong object. Use `--no-rewrite` for a fine-tune, which only follows its recorded texts. |
+| `prompt_prefix` "no power" sentence | hybrid-harness | Dream refuses cable work without it. **Keep it only while it is literally true** (the station C notes say the strip is unplugged). |
+| `reset_first`, auto `/reset` after `SVD did not converge` (max 2) | hybrid-harness | Both grippers start open, and it recovers from Dream's IK error. Now goes through the motion checks (below). |
+| `requires_stage`, relative ball stages, `ARCHIVE_WAIT_S` 4 | hybrid-harness | Balls only after the tower passes. "One more ball" stages are counted from the start of that stage. |
+| Operator console | hybrid-harness | **Practice only.** Off under `--match`. |
+| LiveKit observer | codex/hill-climb-livekit | +10 for showing it to the referee. Read-only. Ported without that branch's plan rewrites. |
+| `--match` | new here | It makes the DQ rules something the harness checks, so nobody has to remember them on the day. |
+
+## Fixes the merge needed
+
+- **`/reset` bypassed the motion checks.** hybrid's `_reset_and_wait` posted `/reset` directly, so an operator stop
+  or a tripped guard didn't block it. `/reset` moves both arms and can't be cancelled once it starts. It now goes
+  through `_motion`, and it is skipped (`reset_skipped` event) while the live guard is armed.
+  `tests/test_final_harness.py` covers both.
+- Conflicts in `cli.py`, `plan.py` and `runner.py` were additive. `_PLAN_KEYS` is the union of both branches.
+
+## Left out
+
+| Idea | Where | Why not |
+|---|---|---|
+| `hybrid_v4_long.yaml` as the match plan | hybrid-harness | In the sim it lost 34 of 185 matches to an 8-ball and ended early in 134 of 185. Keep it for practice comparisons. |
+| Operator console, `--budget`, `monitor+manual` in a match | hybrid / main | Any human input after go is a DQ. `--match` refuses them. |
+| `hill_climb_full` / `hill_climb_sprint` rewrites | codex/hill-climb-livekit | `hill_climb_v4.yaml` and `hybrid_v4.yaml` already follow rubric v4. |
+| `decide.py` making decisions | start-pose-live-guard | No match evidence that it beats the plan order yet. Shadow mode first. |
+| The LiveKit branch's `required_cameras` plan field | codex/hill-climb-livekit | It needs that branch's plan and frames changes. The observer works without it. |
+
+## Stress results (virtual-clock sim, `experiments/stress_match.py` scenarios, 5 seeds each)
+
+37 scenarios × 5 seeds = 185 matches per config. Each scenario is a table the harness has to cope with: refusals,
+frozen or empty cameras, API outages, false "done" verdicts, an 8-ball near the bin, a knocky tower, and so on.
+"fast" means a judge call takes 4–6 s; otherwise 6.5–13 s (Haiku as measured). "final" is this merge with
+`--live-guard` and gripper sensors.
+
+| config | mean pts | lost to DQ | lost to 8-ball | mean balls |
+|---|---|---|---|---|
+| main, v4 plan | 60.5 | 4 | 0 | 0.00 |
+| hybrid, hybrid plan | 61.2 | 4 | 1 | 0.09 |
+| hybrid, hybrid plan, fast | 70.5 | 4 | **28** | 3.01 |
+| hybrid, long plan, fast | 63.2 | 5 | **34** | 3.38 |
+| **final, v4 plan, fast** | 66.6 | 6 | **0** | 2.37 |
+| **final, hybrid plan, fast** | **69.8** | 4 | **0** | 2.90 |
+
+- **The live guard is what makes a fast judge safe.** Without it, a fast judge reaches the balls and loses 28 of
+  185 matches to the 8-ball. With it, none are lost, at a cost of 0.7 points on average.
+- At Haiku's real latency, the live guard and the per-poll guard score the same (`guard_v4` and `guard_v4_live`
+  match row for row). Neither reaches the ball stage, so there is no 8-ball to stop.
+- The 4 DQs every config shares are all `reach_across` (see below).
+- **Open risk, v4 plan with a fast judge:** in 2 matches (`false_done_unplug` and `false_done_all`, same seed),
+  two false "cable out" verdicts landed seconds apart and passed `confirm: 2`. The robot then touched a block.
+  With two calls in flight, the confirming verdicts are close in time and can share the same error. A minimum gap
+  between confirming verdicts would close this. Not done yet.
+
+**This branch as committed**, at Haiku's real latency (6.5–13 s a call), with `--live-guard` and gripper sensors,
+on 8 of the scenarios (40 matches each):
+
+| scenario | main, v4 | this branch, v4 | hybrid, hybrid plan | this branch, hybrid plan |
+|---|---|---|---|---|
+| happy | 74.2 | 75.4 | 74.4 | 75.6 |
+| realistic | 47.6 | 57.6 | 60.0 | 59.2 |
+| realistic_faults | 44.6 | 46.4 | 45.6 | 44.0 |
+| calibrated_base | 35.4 | 32.6 | 49.4 | 54.0 |
+| calibrated_base_faults | 28.4 | 36.2 | 31.8 | 31.8 |
+| knocky_tower | 58.0 | 61.6 | 59.8 | 63.2 |
+| black_heavy_lagged | 89.2 | 90.4 | 90.0 | 87.8 |
+| unplug_never | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
+
+No matches were lost in either config. The stitched, two-in-flight judge in the v4 plan is worth about +10 on
+`realistic`. On `calibrated_base` (base Dream-1's real unplug rate), the hybrid plan beats the v4 plan by about 20
+points.
+
+`hybrid_v4.yaml` needs `--skip cable_extra` in a match. Without it, the cable 3 and 4 stages take about 90 s, and
+the same 8 scenarios score 33.5 instead of 52.0.
+
+## Not solved by the harness
+
+- **Stuck cable / never unplugs:** 0–10 points in every config. Base Dream-1 unplugs about 1 try in 6
+  (TELEOP_PLAN). The sim's default of 0.8 per try is too optimistic, so treat the `calibrated_*` rows as the
+  realistic ones. A fine-tune for the unplug is worth more than any harness change.
+- **`reach_across`:** the robot touches a later-stage object early and gets a DQ in every config (4 of 5 seeds).
+  The guard sees it only after it happens.
+- **Judge latency:** with Haiku at 6.5–13 s a call, even two calls in flight rarely reach the ball stage. Balls
+  showed up only at 4–6 s a call.

@@ -249,6 +249,10 @@ guard:
 - It only sees what the monitor sees, at the monitor's pace: one answer per `check_every_s`, none during `settle_s` after a prompt change. It is a backstop, not a reflex. A team member still watches, ready to call "stop".
 - `max_blind_s: 20` also stops the run when a guarded stage has had no yes/no answer that long (Claude API down, bad frames).
 - **Local detector** (`--detect plans/table_colors.yaml`): `harness/detect.py` checks the wrist frames on every poll, with no API call, and counts an illegal ball (white, or black) between the fingers as a guard yes. It also draws labelled boxes (ball / cube / lying stick, by colour) on the frames the monitor sees. Dream's inputs and the prompts are unchanged. Re-tune the colours on the station's own cameras: `python -m harness.detect plans/table_colors.yaml frame.jpg --out boxed.jpg`.
+- **Live guard** (`--detect plans/table_colors.yaml --live-guard`): runs the same illegal-ball check in its own thread on live wrist frames from the station add-on (`GET /frame/wrist_l.jpg`, `/frame/wrist_r.jpg`, 30 fps, works idle or running) at 10 looks a second (`--live-guard-hz`). Three yes answers in a row on distinct frames (`--live-guard-frames`) POST `/stop` at once, without waiting for the next poll, and the match ends as for any guard trip. Without it the guard sees debug-view trips (one every ~3.4 s on the station) once per poll (3 s in v4), so a held ball could be judged up to ~6 s late. Both wrist views take ~76 ms to check on the laptop. At startup it probes `/frame` and warns if no wrist frame comes back (no add-on, other camera names); it keeps trying, so a camera that comes up later is used. The per-poll guard keeps running either way as a fallback. Yes answers count per camera and only on new frames, so a frozen camera reads as blind (`max_blind_s` stops the run) rather than as all clear. The guard never waits on the runner: it sends `/stop` the moment it trips. The runner checks for a trip (and an operator stop and the deadline) before every /start, /prompt and /home, and again after; a trip it finds afterwards may have been overtaken by its own command, so it sends `/stop` again. That leaves a window of milliseconds in which a /start or /prompt can follow the guard's /stop; the second /stop lands before Dream-1's first action chunk (~3 s for a fresh start on the station). `/home` can't be stopped once begun, so it is refused outright while the live guard is armed (ball stages only; homing only happens before unplug). Both wrist cameras are fetched in parallel with a 0.25 s timeout, so a dead camera doesn't slow the live one. The per-poll guard also counts only new frames now, so a cached debug-view trip can't confirm a yes or keep `max_blind_s` satisfied.
+- **Start pose** (`start_pose:` in the plan): before each stage and each retry the runner logs how far the arms' joints are from the starts recorded in the teleop data for that exact prompt (`start_pose` events in events.jsonl; reference `plans/start_poses.json`, rebuilt with `python tools/start_poses.py plans/start_poses.json <dataset dirs>`). Why: an unfamiliar start pose costs a VLA more than anything else measured in LIBERO-Plus (-60 points vs -16 for reworded instructions). On `home_before` stages (v4: `unplug_cable`) a start farther than `tolerance_rad` (0.35 rad on any joint) stops the run, homes the arms and starts fresh, once per stage, and only if the home pose is itself a recorded start for that prompt. `/home` can't be cancelled once started (the station refuses `/stop` while homing), so it is skipped with less than 30 s of match left, and an operator stop or guard trip during the stop holds the arms where they are instead of homing. Joint angles come from `GET /arms` `positions_rad` (new in `station_addons/sitecustomize.py`; the station must be restarted with it) or, while a run is going, from the newest trip. With neither, the check logs `no_state` and does nothing. Only unplug and put-down have recorded starts so far; stacking and balls log `no_reference`.
+- **Cable ends** (`--detect plans/table_colors.yaml --cable-ends models/cable_end.onnx`): `harness/cable_end.py` finds compact orange blobs (the anodised plug ferrules) and labels each crop USB-C end / USB-A end / neither with a small CNN (MobileNetV3-small, 6 MB ONNX, run by OpenCV; no torch at runtime). The monitor's frames get MAGENTA boxes on USB-C ends and AMBER boxes on USB-A ends (the end that goes into the power strip). Dream's inputs and the prompts are unchanged. Trained on 891 hand-labelled crops from the teleop videos and the table photos (`tools/cable_end/train.py`). Held out by video (7 Nov): 94% accuracy; at the 0.7 confidence it reports at, USB-C precision 0.70 / recall 0.82 and USB-A 0.96 / 0.84 (17 USB-C and 51 USB-A test crops, so treat these as rough). Blender renders of the cable (`tools/cable_end/render_cable.py`, random pose, lighting, lens, power strip, distractors) did not help: synthetic + real scored 92% and lower on the phone photos, so the shipped model is real-only. Try it on images: `python -m harness.cable_end models/cable_end.onnx frame.jpg --out-dir boxed/`.
+- **Decision equations, shadow mode** (`harness/decide.py`, optional `decide:` in the plan): the runner's done / fail rules written as weighted sums with thresholds, plus a planner that gives each stage the timeout worth the most points for the time left. On every look the runner logs a `decide` event: the done score, the fail score and its terms, and what the equations would decide. They never act; with no `decide:` section they decide exactly as the runner does (tests check this on 5,000 random look sequences), so any difference in the log comes from weights you set, e.g. `decide: {done: {theta: 1.0}}` or `decide: {fail: {combine: sum}}`. Stage success curves for the planner: `python tools/fit_stage_curves.py runs/ --out plans/stage_curves.json` (censored maximum likelihood over each stage's attempts). `plans/stage_curves_sim.json` is from Dream's YAM sim only (8 Nov): unplug p_max 0.64, no success before ~23 s, median 56 s; put-down and stacking never succeeded there, so fit station runs before trusting any timeout. Background: `docs/DECISION_MODEL.md`.
 
 ### Other stage fields added 8 Nov
 - `confirm: 1` on a stage overrides the plan's `confirm` (for stages where a false "done" costs nothing).
@@ -297,6 +301,65 @@ dimos mcp list-tools                  # in another terminal
 
 **Rules:** an agent choosing stages live is not a human input. Still, check with the organizers before letting any model other than Dream-1 decide prompts in a match.
 
+## 6g. Zero-shot base Dream-1: `plans/hybrid_v4.yaml`
+
+Same match structure as `hill_climb_v4.yaml`, written for base Dream-1 instead of a fine-tune:
+
+- **Specific prompts** naming objects by colour and position ("pick up the pink cube ... onto the blue cube"),
+  and `tower_prompts` with `<piece>` and `<top>` so each stacking prompt names the next piece and the one below it.
+- **`rewrite_prompts: true`**: the monitor may answer `next_prompt`, and the runner sends it as the robot's next
+  instruction (at most `REWRITE_MAX` = 3 an attempt, `REWRITE_COOLDOWN_S` = 15 s apart). `prompt_prefix` is put back
+  on a rewrite when the stage's own prompt opens with it (Dream refuses cable work without the "no power" sentence).
+  `system_prompt` holds the station lessons the monitor follows. Logged as `prompt_rewrite`.
+- **`reset_first: true`**: `/reset` and wait for idle before the first stage, so both grippers start open.
+- After Dream's IK error (`SVD did not converge`) the restart is preceded by `/reset` + idle, at most
+  `AUTO_RESET_MAX` = 2 a run (`auto_reset` events).
+- Gripper sensors (`GET /arms`) overrule the monitor's `holding`: a gripper closed fully on nothing is reported as
+  holding nothing, whatever the images seem to show.
+- Frames come from the station's `/frame/<cam>.jpg?w=640` by default (~35 KB a camera, not the debug view's ~2 MB
+  round trips that slowed Dream to 2-6 s); `HARNESS_FRAMES=view` forces the debug view, which is also the fallback.
+- `--skip cable_` drops the cable section for stack + balls practice.
+- **Fine-tune labels must match the prompts we settle on** (stage prompts, tower prompts, logged rewrites).
+
+### `plans/hybrid_v4_long.yaml`: Dream-1's own prompt style
+
+Dream Labs' examples for Dream-1 are short imperatives that each carry a whole task ("Stack the cubes into a single
+tower", "Pick up all markers one by one and put into black pencil holder using both arms"; dreamlabs.ai/research/dream-1).
+This plan has 6 stages instead of 20: one short prompt each for a cable, the whole tower, the stick on top and all the
+balls, with long timeouts and the monitor stepping in only when the robot is on the wrong thing. Run both plans on the
+same table and keep what scores.
+
+### Talking to it while it runs (testing only)
+
+Type plain words and Enter at any time: "skip the next step", "why did it grab the pink one?", "lower the blocks
+more slowly next time". Claude reads each line with the run so far and the newest frames, answers in the terminal,
+and may end the current stage, skip later ones, send the robot a new instruction now, or keep a note that the monitor
+follows for the rest of the run. Notes are appended to `--lessons` (default `lessons/operator.md`) and read into the
+monitor's system prompt on later runs. Everything is in `events.jsonl` (`operator_input`, `operator_reply`,
+`operator_action`) and on the trace page (`python tools/trace_report.py`). `s` + Enter still stops. Any human input
+during a match is a DQ: run matches with `--match` (it turns the operator off; see [FINAL_HARNESS.md](FINAL_HARNESS.md)).
+
+## 6h. Observe a stage through LiveKit (`python -m harness.livekit_observer`)
+
+The rubric gives +10 for showing LiveKit use to the referee before the match. The observer subscribes to the station's
+camera tracks, never publishes, and isn't part of the runner: the runner still verifies its own stages from the
+station's frames.
+
+Install `livekit==1.2.1` (not in the base requirements). Set `LIVEKIT_URL` and `LIVEKIT_TOKEN` for the station's room,
+with a token that has `canSubscribe` on and `canPublish` and `canPublishData` off.
+
+```bash
+python -m harness.livekit_observer --publisher metal-station \
+  --track overhead=table_top --track wrist_l=left_wrist \
+  --plan plans/hill_climb_v4.yaml --stage tower_pass
+```
+
+Use the publisher identity and exact video track names from the room. With `--plan` and `--stage`, each sample also
+gets an advisory verdict from the Claude monitor (needs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`). Leave both out to capture camera evidence
+only. JPEGs and verdicts go to `runs_livekit/`. `receive_age_ms` is the time since this subscriber got the frame; the
+RTC timestamp is logged separately. A sample is taken only when every named track has a frame under `--max-age` (1 s)
+that wasn't in the previous sample.
+
 ## 7. Test in simulation (before the robot)
 
 `sim_station.py` serves the same API (8091) and debug view (8093), driven by Dream's MuJoCo YAM simulator (`dream_examples/yam_mujoco`). The harness can't tell it apart from a real station, and nothing physical moves, so `--live` is safe here. Note that the Hill Climb uses Metal arms, while the sim is YAM only. That makes it a test of the harness logic and prompts, not of the real task.

+# Operator decisions (Darsh, 8 Nov 2026)
+
+Handwritten notes: [decisions.pdf](decisions.pdf). Below is a typed transcription, with Darsh's answers to the
+unclear spots in brackets. These notes are for the **real table**: coloured golf balls (green, yellow, orange) are
+legal and the white ball is illegal. In the sim the colours differ (white golf balls legal, black 8-balls illegal),
+and the sim plans translate them.
+
+## Blocks
+
+- Come down on the block from the top, normal to the table.
+- Invoke the other arm as a watcher first, then do the cylinders. [It reads "watcher", not "doesn't work".]
+- Place the square blocks (the ones with a round hole) with the hole facing sideways (circle plane normal to the
+  table).
+- Place each square block directly on top of the previous one, minimising overhang.
+- On the last block, turn the hole to face up (circle parallel to the table). Then put a cylinder in the hole,
+  minimising the cylinder's angle.
+- Pick the cylinder up normal to the table, then turn it lengthwise vertical and place it through the hole, at 90°
+  to the table (vertical).
+- Then stack the next cylinder on top, minimising vibration so the tower doesn't topple.
+
+## Golf balls
+
+- Push the white balls off the table.
+- If other objects are in the way, avoid the white balls. Pick up the coloured balls and drop them into the basket.
+
+## Cables
+
+- Use the other arm to watch the cables from a different angle.
+- Come in normal to the cables and grip at max strength.
+- Orient the cables vertical, then pull.
+- Grip 2–3 cables at a time.
+- Drop or lay the cables flat near the power hub (the big black power strip).
+
+## Power outlet
+
+- Pick up a USB-C cable connector by the black part, normal to the table, at peak strength.
+- Come down to the USB-C port (the first port from the left side), normal to the port.
+- Plug it in by bringing the arm vertically down.
+- Confirm the cable is plugged in, then open the gripper and disengage.
+
+## Error detection
+
+- Detect problems.
+
+## Decisions
+
+- If a task fails more than twice, move on.
+- Allow up to three tries if the task is worth a lot of points.

+# Harness decision model: runtime, arms, balls, prompts, and the decision tree as tunable equations
+
+7 Nov 2026 (late). This builds on `HARNESS_SPEED_STATE_SPACE_2026-11-07.md`. It uses:
+- today's inference logs from the NUC (1,650 chunks over 52 runs);
+- the runner on `main` / `start-pose-live-guard`;
+- kaupau's `hybrid-harness` branch, which does prompt generation better (section 4);
+- the sequential vs combined two-arm benchmark (`codex/dual-arm-experiment`, `runs_dual_arm/`).
+
+## 0. Findings that drive the design
+
+| # | Finding | Evidence |
+|---|---|---|
+| F1 | **The arms sit still about 30% of the time.** Dream's pacer runs `sync` whenever the median round trip is over 900 ms: it waits for each 2.4 s chunk, and chunks arrive every 3.54 s (p50). In `async` mode chunks arrive every 1.55 s with no gap. 65% of today's chunks were sync. | `robots-inference.jsonl`; station.log `dream.pacing: pacing sync ... over the 900 ms threshold` |
+| F2 | The round trip is about 950 ms even with a fast upload (≈650 ms of gateway overhead), and upload size makes it worse. Each request is ~1.6 MB. With a 5 Mbps uplink (p10) the round trip is ~1.9 s, and only 4% of those chunks could run async. | same log, `request_bytes`, `up_mbps` |
+| F3 | A fresh `/start` costs 2.9 s (p50) before motion begins; `/prompt` on a running session costs nothing extra. | run_start → chunk 1 |
+| F4 | The harness costs about 5-8 s per stage boundary: 3 s poll, ~4 s monitor call, then confirm 2 means one more look. | runner constants, stress sim |
+| F5 | **Dream-1 does not reliably obey arm naming.** "with the left arm, …" succeeded 0/4 and the right arm still moved 10-19 s. "Left does X while right does Y" succeeded 0/4. Two goals without naming arms finished one goal in 2/4 trials. (YAM sim, n = 12.) | `runs_dual_arm/20261107T230412Z/results.jsonl` |
+| F6 | Base Dream-1 follows short, specific prompts that name objects by colour and position much better than the generic task texts. It refuses cable work without the "no power" sentence (4 × DA-4000 today). | `hybrid-harness` plan notes, inference log errors |
+| F7 | The harness's own 2 MB debug-view frame fetches slowed Dream to 2-6 s per chunk. The add-on's `/frame/<cam>.jpg?w=640` frames are ~35 KB. | `hybrid-harness` HARNESS_GUIDE 6g |
+
+## 1. Runtime: where the seconds go, ranked by expected gain
+
+Model of one stage: `T_stage = T_start + T_robot / u + T_detect + T_decide`, where `u` is the share of time the arms actually move (F1).
+
+| Lever | Saves | Cost / risk | How to measure |
+|---|---|---|---|
+| **L1. Get Dream into async.** Wired ethernet on the NUC. Stop the harness pulling debug-view trips (use hybrid's light frames, F7). Ask Dream whether the 900 ms pacing threshold or the image size per request can be configured. | u ≈ 0.70 → ~0.95, about 25% less robot time on every stage | network work only; any station config change is the station owner's decision | share of `async` chunks and chunk-gap p50 in `robots-inference.jsonl`, before vs after |
+| **L2. Event-driven stage done.** A local estimator (gripper sensors from `/arms`, detector boxes, cable-end boxes) advances when its posterior is high. The monitor confirms in parallel and can veto. | 4-7 s per boundary, 20-35 s per match | a false done on a pass bar is a DQ, so it is gated by the bound in section 5.3 | `stage_start` − true-done time in replayed logs |
+| **L3. Never `/start` again mid-match.** Recoveries and retries use `/prompt`, not stop + start. | 2.9 s each time | none; it's already mostly the case | count `start` calls per match |
+| **L4. Skip checks the sensors can answer.** "Are both grippers empty?" (release_cable, arms_clear, put-downs) comes from `/arms` gripper readings in milliseconds, not a 4 s synchronous VLM look. Hybrid already lets the sensors overrule the monitor's `holding`. | ~4 s per skippable stage, 3-4 per match | sensor calibration (hybrid has this station's readings) | `entry_check` latency |
+| **L5. Faster judge.** Use hybrid's stitched grid, two staggered calls in flight, and 4 history frames. | a verdict every ~5 s instead of ~10 s | merge from hybrid | `latency_s` in verdict events |
+
+L1 and L2 dominate. L1 is a network and ops change, not code.
+
+## 2. Both arms at once
+
+The station API has **one prompt for one bimanual policy**. There is no per-arm command and no way to run two sessions. So "both arms at the same time" has to come from one of these:
+
+| Mode | How | Works today? | Use for |
+|---|---|---|---|
+| **A. One worker, the other parked (current).** | The right arm does everything; the left is parked by the reset prompt. | yes | default |
+| **B. Watcher.** | The stage's `watch:` puts one arm's wrist camera on the work, and the other arm works. The harness re-sends the park prompt if the watcher drifts (`watch_lost`). | partly: depends on Dream holding the watcher still (F5 says naming the arm is unreliable) | close views of the port, the ball in the gripper, the tower top |
+| **C. Combined prompt, two goals.** | "put the X … and the Y …" without naming arms; Dream assigns them. | 2/4 single-goal successes in the sim, both arms moving at once | parallel independent goals, e.g. ball + ball, once tested on Metal |
+| **D. Trained bimanual skill.** | Record teleop demos where the left arm watches or helps while the right works, under one fixed task text. | needs data | the only route to dependable two-arm behaviour |
+
+The harness can't make the two arms independent tasks. It **can** decide which mode each stage uses, which arm is the worker (section 3), and when a combined prompt is worth the risk. That decision gets a weight like the others (section 5).
+
+Experiment E1 (sim first, then station): 3 modes × 20 trials each on the same seeded scenes. Measure success, time to done, both-arms-moving seconds, and collisions or knocks. 20 per mode separates 30% from 70% success. A 0.5 vs 0.8 gap needs about 40 per mode for 80% power.
+
+## 3. Picking the arm from the layout
+
+What the harness has: the overhead camera, colour detector boxes (balls, cubes, sticks), cable-end boxes (new), gripper state, and joints (`/arms`, after a station restart).
+
+1. **Table frame.** One homography H from overhead pixels to table metres. Click the four table corners once per station (or mark them with tags) and store it in the plan. The arm bases `b_L` and `b_R` and the bin, hub and tower positions all go in that frame.
+2. **Cost of arm a for object o:**
+
+       c_a(o) = w_reach · ‖p_o − b_a‖ / R_a             reach, normalised by the arm's reach
+              + w_cross · 1[o is past the midline for a]   crossing the body
+              + w_clear · Σ_k exp(−d(path_a,o, k)² / σ²)   obstacles near the straight path: tower, hub, illegal balls
+              + w_busy  · 1[a holds something or is the watcher]
+              + w_skill · (1 − s_a(task))                  arm-specific skill prior: demos exist only for the right arm
+
+   Choose `a* = argmin_a c_a(o)`. If `c_a*(o) > c_max` for every arm, the object is unreachable: skip it or pick another object.
+3. **Turning the choice into an action.** F5 means naming the arm in the prompt is not enough on its own. In order of dependability:
+   - (i) choose the **object** the chosen arm is best placed for, and name it by colour and position ("the orange ball nearest the right arm"). Dream tends to use the nearer arm;
+   - (ii) for a fine-tune, record per-arm task texts ("… nearest the left arm" as well as "… nearest the right arm");
+   - (iii) the arm phrase as a hint only, and the monitor checks which arm moved. Count a wrong-arm move as a failed attempt.
+4. Experiment E2: does base Dream-1 on Metal pick the nearer arm? Place objects at 5 positions from left to right, 4 trials each, with and without an arm phrase. Log which arm moved first. The result sets `w_skill` and tells us whether (iii) is worth anything.
+
+## 4. Prompting Dream
+
+**Build on kaupau's `hybrid-harness` branch.** What it does that main doesn't:
+- **Templates with slots:** `tower_prompts` with `<piece>` / `<top>`, cable prompts as plan variables, and a `prompt_prefix` that is put back on every rewrite.
+- **Closed-loop rewriting:** the monitor returns `next_prompt` when it sees a wrong object or step, a plain stall, or a failure about to happen. The runner sends it, with a cooldown of 15 s and at most 3 per attempt. Every rewrite is logged as `prompt_rewrite`.
+- **Station lessons in the monitor's system prompt:** plugs are pulled straight up; carry a block high and lower it straight down; Dream ignores fine adjustments like "1 cm higher". There is also a running operator-lessons file, for testing only.
+- **`tools/label_rollout.py`:** labels Dream's own run records with stage, attempt, outcome and the clean task text. This is the bridge to fine-tune data.
+
+Formalised: the prompt is a policy `π_P(x) = template_s(slots(x))`, with `rewrite(x)` as a gated fallback. Slots come from the state estimate: object colour and position, arm (section 3), next tower piece, and which plug is next. A rewrite fires only when
+
+    g_rw = w_wrong·1[wrong object/step] + w_stall·(t − t_rise)/stall_s + w_risk·P(knock) − w_churn·n_rewrites  >  θ_rw
+    and  t − t_last_prompt ≥ τ_cool
+
+Hybrid's fixed rules are one point in this space: `θ_rw` = "the monitor chose to write one", τ_cool = 15, and a hard cap of 3.
+
+**Prompt experiments (E3).** The unit is one stage from a seeded scene, scored on success within T, time to first contact, and refusals.
+- Factors: specificity (generic task text vs colour + position), motion adverbs ("lift high, lower straight down"), arm phrase (none / hint), preamble (on / off, cables only), and length.
+- Plan: a fractional factorial screen in the YAM sim with real Dream-1, which is cheap. Then confirm the best 2-3 on Metal at 10-20 trials each.
+- Prompts already sent today, from the inference log, cover about 40 variants across 52 runs, but the log has no outcomes. Join them to harness `events.jsonl` with `label_rollout.py` to get a free first ranking.
+
+The fine-tune rule still holds. A fine-tune follows only its recorded texts, so whatever the base-model experiments settle on is what the teleop labels must say. Hybrid runs a fine-tune with `--no-rewrite` plus the recorded texts.
+
+## 5. The decision tree as weighted equations
+
+### 5.1 What the runner does today, as rules
+
+At each check (every `check_every_s` = 3 s), per stage attempt:
+- **done** if the verifier says done `confirm` (2) times in a row, or twice within `DONE_WINDOW_S` (15 s), or with `immediate` set;
+- **fail** if `failing` comes `confirm` times in a row and elapsed ≥ `FAIL_MIN_FRACTION` (0.5) × `timeout_s`; or progress drops by `REGRESSION_DROP` (0.3) below the attempt's max; or progress hasn't risen by `STALL_RISE` (0.05) for `stall_s`; or `timeout_s` passes, with one extension of `EXTEND_S` (20 s) while the gripper is on the target and `CONFIRM_GRACE_S` (10 s) while a done is pending;
+- **on fail:** abort, skip, or the recovery menu in order (`max_uses` each), then `home_and_retry` if allowed, up to `max_attempts`, never with less than `HOME_RESERVE_S` (30 s) left;
+- **guard trips** after `guard.confirm` illegal-ball answers (live: 3 distinct frames at 10 Hz), or when blind for `max_blind_s`.
+
+### 5.2 The same tree as equations
+
+State `x_t`: stage s, elapsed τ, time left `L = T − t`, the evidence features `φ(t)`, attempt count n, recovery uses, and the arm and object state.
+
+**Done evidence (log-odds, recursive):**
+
+    ℓ_t = γ · ℓ_{t−1} + Σ_i w_i · φ_i(t)
+
+| φ_i | Source | Today's implicit weight |
+|---|---|---|
+| monitor done (+1) / not (−1), × confidence | VLM | +1 / reset to 0 |
+| progress p_t (0..1) | VLM | used only for stall and regression |
+| gripper went from closed on an object to open, near the target | `/arms` | none (new) |
+| object at goal (detector box inside the bin or on the tower) | detector | none (new) |
+| plug count in the hub fell (cable-end boxes) | classifier | none (new) |
+| same frames as last time | sha1 | φ = 0 (not counted) |
+
+Done when `ℓ_t ≥ θ_done`. Today's rule is the special case γ = 1 on agreement and 0 on disagreement, `w_monitor = 1`, `θ_done = confirm = 2`.
+
+**Fail hazard:**
+
+    h_t = σ( b + w_f·n_fail + w_r·max(0, p_max − p_t − δ) + w_s·(τ − τ_rise)/stall_s + w_T·τ/timeout_s − w_on·1[on target] )
+
+The attempt ends when `h_t > θ_fail`. The hard rules above are the corners of this function: they fire when any one term is large.
+
+**Action choice when an attempt ends (or at each tick, for `continue`):** a ∈ {continue, re-prompt / rewrite, reset prompt, home + retry, skip, abort}.
+
+    Q(a | x) = P_a(succ within c_a) · V_s  +  W(L − c_a)  −  λ_risk · R_a
+
+- `V_s` = the stage's points (rubric v4: first cable +10, extra cable +5, tower pass +20, +3 per inch, ball +5), plus the value it unlocks (pass bars gate later stages).
+- `W(L)` = expected points from the stages after s with L seconds left. Compute it backwards over the stage list once per plan, using each stage's success-vs-time curve `F_s(τ)`.
+- `c_a` = time the action costs (re-prompt ~1 s, reset 5-20 s, home ~10 s plus `/start` 2.9 s).
+- `R_a` = risk: a DQ from a false done or a touch out of order, a knocked tower, an illegal ball.
+
+Pick `a* = argmax Q`. `on_fail: skip` and `max_attempts` become consequences of Q, not hand-set values.
+
+**Optimal timeout (replaces a fixed `timeout_s`):** keep going on stage s while the success rate per second, times its value, beats what the remaining time is worth elsewhere:
+
+    f_s(τ) / (1 − F_s(τ)) · V_s   ≥   dW/dL
+
+That gives each stage a timeout that depends on its own success curve and on how much match is left. Optional stages get short timeouts late in the match and longer ones early.
+
+### 5.3 The pass-bar constraint
+
+A false done on a pass bar (unplug, stack_2, stack_3, tower_pass) is a DQ. With per-look false-positive rate α for the evidence and k independent looks required, `P(false done) ≈ α^k`. Choose `θ_done` per stage so that `P(false done) ≤ ε` (e.g. 1%), then minimise the expected detection delay. This is the one hard constraint in the tuning. Everything else trades off in `J` below.
+
+### 5.4 Parameters and tuning
+
+θ = {w_i, γ, θ_done(s), w_f, w_r, w_s, w_T, w_on, θ_fail, λ_risk, θ_rw, w_wrong, w_churn, τ_cool, c_a weights (section 3), check period, live-guard Hz and frames, start-pose tolerance}. Today's constants (`confirm`, `STALL_RISE`, `REGRESSION_DROP`, `FAIL_MIN_FRACTION`, `DONE_WINDOW_S`, `EXTEND_S`, `CONFIRM_GRACE_S`, `HOME_RESERVE_S`, `SKIP_MIN_CONFIDENCE`, `REWRITE_*`) are its starting point.
+
+Objective, estimated in the stress sim and the rubric-v4 referee:
+
+    J(θ) = E[score] − μ · P(DQ) − ν · P(illegal ball)
+
+1. **Calibrate the sim's evidence model from real logs.** Per stage, how often the monitor says done when it's not, and the reverse (label saved frames by hand). Also the detector's miss and false-alarm rates, `F_s(τ)` from the runs, and refusal and IK-error rates. This is the STEP / SIMPLER warning: tuning against a sim with the wrong noise picks the wrong θ.
+2. **Search:** CMA-ES or coordinate hill climbing over θ. Use common random numbers (the same seeded scenes for every candidate), about 200 sim matches per candidate, run on the cluster.
+3. **Confirm on Metal:** the best 2 θ vs today's, 5 supervised matches each. Promote only if J is better and P(DQ) didn't rise.
+
+## 6. Ball handling
+
+Golf ball: 42.7 mm, rolls when nudged. The bin is white, the same colour as the illegal white ball. Dream does the grasp. The harness controls **which ball, from where, with what prompt, and whether the grasp worked**:
+
+1. **Choose the ball** (overhead detector + homography): legal colour; reachable by the chosen arm (section 3); clearance from the tower `d_tower ≥ 2·D_ball` and from any illegal ball `d_illegal ≥ 1.5·D_ball` so the gripper can't take both; not against an edge it can roll off. Score `s(b) = −c_a*(b) − w_tw·exp(−d_tower²/σ²) − w_il·exp(−d_illegal²/σ²)` and send the best: "pick up the orange ball nearest the right arm, straight down from above, and drop it into the white bin".
+2. **Grasp check, local and fast.** After the gripper closes, its opening g from `/arms` should sit in a band around the ball's width, `g ∈ [g_ball − δ, g_ball + δ]`, for N ticks. Fully closed means a miss; wider means it's on something else. This is the same idea as hybrid's "closed fully on nothing" sensor rule. Calibrate `g_ball` once by closing on a ball.
+3. **Rolled away:** if the target's overhead position jumps by more than D_ball between frames without being held, re-target with a new prompt naming the ball's new position. Don't retry blind.
+4. **The illegal ball:** the live guard (10 Hz, `/stop` in under 0.5 s) stays as the hard stop. The ball choice in step 1 lowers the chance it's ever needed.
+5. **Dream's own ball skill:** there are no ball demos yet; balls rely on base Dream-1. To make it dependable, record ball teleop (top-down grasp, carry high, release over the bin) under one fixed text, and add one demo set starting next to the white ball so the policy learns to go around it.
+
+## 7. What to build, in order
+
+1. Merge `hybrid-harness` (prompting, light frames, faster judge, sensor overrides) with `start-pose-live-guard` (start pose, live guard, cable-end classifier).
+2. **L1 network check:** ethernet, and light frames only. Re-measure the async share. Ops only; the biggest single gain.
+3. Overhead homography + arm/ball choice (sections 3 and 6) as a `layout.py` that feeds prompt slots.
+4. A `decide.py` with the equations in 5.2. Default weights reproduce today's rules exactly, with a test proving it. Then calibrate and tune in the sim (5.4).
+5. Experiments E1 (dual-arm modes), E2 (nearer-arm choice), E3 (prompt factors), sim first, then Metal.

@@ -0,0 +1,163 @@
+"""Which end of the orange cable is where: USB-C end, USB-A end, or neither, from any camera frame.
+
+Two steps, numpy + OpenCV only (no torch at runtime):
+1. Candidates: compact orange blobs, i.e. the anodised ferrules (the cable is a long thin run, the strain-relief
+   ribs are slivers). Skin, faces and the orange golf ball also pass; step 2 rejects them.
+2. A small CNN (MobileNetV3-small, 96 x 96 crop, ~6 MB ONNX run by cv2.dnn) labels each crop usb_c / usb_a / none.
+   Trained on Blender renders of this cable (tools/cable_end/render_cable.py: random pose, lighting, lens,
+   backgrounds from station frames) plus hand-labelled station crops (tools/cable_end/train.py).
+
+The station's cables are USB-A to USB-C; the USB-A end is the one that goes into the power strip's USB ports.
+Used to draw "USB-C end" / "USB-A end" boxes on the frames the monitor sees, like harness.detect's boxes. It never
+touches Dream's inputs or the prompts.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+from dataclasses import dataclass
+from pathlib import Path
+
+import cv2
+import numpy as np
+
+LABELS = ("usb_c", "usb_a", "none")
+NAMES = {"usb_c": "USB-C end", "usb_a": "USB-A end"}
+WORK_W = 640
+SIZE = 96
+BATCH = 8                 # fixed DNN input batch (see CableEndClassifier._forward)
+CROP_SCALE = 2.6
+MEAN = np.array([0.485, 0.456, 0.406], np.float32)
+STD = np.array([0.229, 0.224, 0.225], np.float32)
+
+
+@dataclass
+class End:
+    label: str            # usb_c | usb_a
+    prob: float
+    x0: float
+    y0: float
+    x1: float
+    y1: float             # fractions of the image
+
+
+def ferrules(bgr: np.ndarray, work_w: int = WORK_W) -> list[dict]:
+    """Candidate ferrules: [{cx, cy, side}] in the input image's pixels (side = crop side)."""
+    h0, w0 = bgr.shape[:2]
+    s = work_w / w0
+    im = cv2.resize(bgr, (work_w, max(1, round(h0 * s))), interpolation=cv2.INTER_AREA)
+    hsv = cv2.cvtColor(im, cv2.COLOR_BGR2HSV)
+    H, S, V = hsv[..., 0], hsv[..., 1], hsv[..., 2]
+    mask = ((H >= 3) & (H <= 22) & (S >= 110) & (V >= 60)).astype(np.uint8)
+    mask = cv2.morphologyEx(mask, cv2.MORPH_CLOSE, np.ones((3, 3), np.uint8))
+    n, lab, stats, _ = cv2.connectedComponentsWithStats(mask)
+    total = im.shape[0] * im.shape[1]
+    out = []
+    for i in range(1, n):
+        area = int(stats[i, cv2.CC_STAT_AREA])
+        if area < 30 or area > 0.08 * total:
+            continue
+        ys, xs = np.nonzero(lab == i)
+        (cx, cy), (w, h), _ = cv2.minAreaRect(np.stack([xs, ys], 1).astype(np.float32))
+        L, W = max(w, h), max(1.0, min(w, h))
+        if L / W > 4.0 or area / max(L * W, 1.0) < 0.5:
+            continue
+        out.append({"cx": cx / s, "cy": cy / s, "side": max(L * CROP_SCALE, 24) / s})
+    return out
+
+
+def crop(bgr: np.ndarray, cand: dict, size: int = SIZE) -> np.ndarray:
+    side = cand["side"]
+    x0, y0 = cand["cx"] - side / 2, cand["cy"] - side / 2
+    M = np.float32([[size / side, 0, -x0 * size / side], [0, size / side, -y0 * size / side]])
+    return cv2.warpAffine(bgr, M, (size, size), flags=cv2.INTER_AREA, borderMode=cv2.BORDER_REPLICATE)
+
+
+def _prep(crops: list[np.ndarray]) -> np.ndarray:
+    x = np.stack([c[:, :, ::-1].astype(np.float32) / 255.0 for c in crops])
+    return ((x - MEAN) / STD).transpose(0, 3, 1, 2).copy()
+
+
+class CableEndClassifier:
+    def __init__(self, model_path: str | Path, min_prob: float = 0.7, max_candidates: int = 24):
+        self.net = cv2.dnn.readNetFromONNX(str(model_path))
+        self.min_prob = min_prob
+        self.max_candidates = max_candidates
+
+    def _forward(self, x: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
+        """Always the same input shape, padded: OpenCV 5.0's DNN kills the process when one net is reused with a
+        batch larger than an earlier one (2, 5, 4, 4, then 16 crashed it, 7 Nov). Chunks of BATCH cost ~2.5 ms."""
+        out = []
+        for k in range(0, len(x), BATCH):
+            chunk = x[k:k + BATCH]
+            pad = np.zeros((BATCH,) + x.shape[1:], np.float32)
+            pad[:len(chunk)] = chunk
+            self.net.setInput(pad)
+            out.append(self.net.forward()[:len(chunk)].copy())
+        return np.concatenate(out)
+
+    def ends(self, bgr: np.ndarray) -> list[End]:
+        cands = ferrules(bgr)[: self.max_candidates]
+        if not cands:
+            return []
+        logits = self._forward(_prep([crop(bgr, c) for c in cands]))
+        e = np.exp(logits - logits.max(1, keepdims=True))
+        probs = e / e.sum(1, keepdims=True)
+        h, w = bgr.shape[:2]
+        out = []
+        for c, p in zip(cands, probs):
+            k = int(p.argmax())
+            if LABELS[k] == "none" or p[k] < self.min_prob:
+                continue
+            half = c["side"] / 2.6 * 0.75     # box around the ferrule and tip, not the whole crop
+            out.append(End(LABELS[k], float(p[k]), max(0, c["cx"] - half) / w, max(0, c["cy"] - half) / h,
+                           min(w, c["cx"] + half) / w, min(h, c["cy"] + half) / h))
+        return out
+
+    def ends_jpeg(self, jpeg: bytes) -> list[End]:
+        bgr = cv2.imdecode(np.frombuffer(jpeg, np.uint8), cv2.IMREAD_COLOR)
+        return [] if bgr is None else self.ends(bgr)
+
+    def annotate(self, jpeg: bytes) -> bytes:
+        bgr = cv2.imdecode(np.frombuffer(jpeg, np.uint8), cv2.IMREAD_COLOR)
+        if bgr is None:
+            return jpeg
+        found = self.ends(bgr)
+        if not found:
+            return jpeg
+        h, w = bgr.shape[:2]
+        th = max(2, w // 300)
+        for e in found:
+            col = (255, 0, 255) if e.label == "usb_c" else (0, 200, 255)   # magenta = USB-C, amber = USB-A
+            p0, p1 = (int(e.x0 * w), int(e.y0 * h)), (int(e.x1 * w), int(e.y1 * h))
+            cv2.rectangle(bgr, p0, p1, col, th)
+            cv2.putText(bgr, f"{NAMES[e.label]} {e.prob:.2f}", (p0[0], max(12, p0[1] - 4)), 0,
+                        max(0.4, w / 1600), col, max(1, th // 2))
+        ok, buf = cv2.imencode(".jpg", bgr, [cv2.IMWRITE_JPEG_QUALITY, 88])
+        return buf.tobytes() if ok else jpeg
+
+    @staticmethod
+    def legend() -> str:
+        return ("MAGENTA boxes = USB-C end of a cable, AMBER boxes = USB-A end (the end that plugs into the power "
+                "strip's USB ports); from a local classifier, can miss or mislabel.")
+
+
+def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
+    import argparse
+    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="label cable ends in images")
+    ap.add_argument("model")
+    ap.add_argument("images", nargs="+")
+    ap.add_argument("--out-dir", default=None)
+    a = ap.parse_args(argv)
+    clf = CableEndClassifier(a.model)
+    for p in a.images:
+        data = Path(p).read_bytes()
+        for e in clf.ends_jpeg(data):
+            print(f"{p}: {NAMES[e.label]} p={e.prob:.2f} box=({e.x0:.2f},{e.y0:.2f})-({e.x1:.2f},{e.y1:.2f})")
+        if a.out_dir:
+            Path(a.out_dir).mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+            (Path(a.out_dir) / (Path(p).stem + "_ends.jpg")).write_bytes(clf.annotate(data))
+    return 0
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    raise SystemExit(main())

     p.add_argument("--detect", default=None, metavar="COLORS.yaml",

     p.add_argument("--verifier", choices=["manual", "claude", "time", "claude+manual", "monitor", "monitor+manual"], default=None,

+    p.add_argument("--skip", action="append", default=[], metavar="PREFIX",
+                   help="drop the stages whose names start with PREFIX, e.g. --skip cable_ (repeatable; practice)")
+    p.add_argument("--checkpoint", default=None, help="override the plan's checkpoint, e.g. a fine-tune")
+    p.add_argument("--no-rewrite", action="store_true",

+    p.add_argument("--match", action="store_true",
+                   help="match mode: no operator console (any human input is a DQ), no --budget override, no manual "
+                        "verifier; operator lessons from practice are still read into the monitor")
     p.add_argument("--var", action="append", default=[], metavar="NAME=VALUE",
                    help="fill a {NAME} placeholder in the plan, e.g. --var color=orange (repeatable)")
     return p
 
 
+MANUAL_VERIFIERS = ("manual", "claude+manual", "monitor+manual")
+
+
+def match_problems(args: argparse.Namespace, plan) -> list[str]:
+    """Why these args and this plan can't run as a match (empty list = fine). Any human input after go is a DQ."""
+    problems = []
+    if args.budget:
+        problems.append("--budget: a match runs on the plan's own time_budget_s")
+    if args.verifier in MANUAL_VERIFIERS:
+        problems.append(f"--verifier {args.verifier}: a typed y/n is human input")
+    manual = sorted({st.name for st in plan.stages if st.verifier in MANUAL_VERIFIERS})
+    if manual and args.verifier not in ("monitor", "claude", "time"):
+        problems.append(f"stages answered by a person: {', '.join(manual)} (use --verifier monitor)")
+    return problems
+

@@ -0,0 +1,337 @@
+"""The runner's decision tree as weighted equations, so its constants can be tuned (HARNESS_DECISION_MODEL section 5).
+
+Three pieces, all pure functions of numbers (no I/O, no clock):
+
+1. Done evidence, a running score over looks:  l_t = keep * l_{t-1} + sum_i w_i * phi_i(t),  done when l_t >= theta.
+   With DEFAULT weights it is exactly the runner's rule: `confirm` monitor "done" looks in a row pass the stage, a
+   "not done" look resets the count, a repeated look at the same frames counts for nothing, and a second "done"
+   within done_window_s passes even with a "failing" flicker in between.
+2. Fail score:  F = combine_k(w_k * g_k), each g_k a ratio that reaches 1 when today's hard rule fires
+   (consecutive failing looks / confirm, consecutive "stalled" looks / confirm, time since progress rose / stall_s,
+   elapsed / timeout_s). A look is failing on a no, a "failing" status, or progress falling REGRESSION_DROP below
+   the attempt's best (look_failing). The attempt fails when F >= theta_fail. combine="max" is today's tree (any one rule);
+   combine="sum" lets weak signals add up, which is what tuning can explore.
+3. Value of the time left:  W_i(L) = max_T  F_i(T) (V_i + W_{i+1}(L - t_succ)) + (1 - F_i(T)) * [skip ? W_{i+1}(L - T) : 0]
+   over a stage list, by dynamic programming on a 1 s grid. It gives each stage the timeout that maximises expected
+   points for the time left (`plan_timeouts`) and the value of an action (`action_value`):
+   Q(a) = P_a * V_s + W(L - c_a) - risk_weight * R_a.
+
+Nothing here moves a robot or changes the runner yet: the runner keeps its own rules. The tests check that the
+default weights reproduce them; tuning and shadow logging come next.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import math
+from dataclasses import dataclass, field, replace
+from typing import Iterable, Sequence
+
+# Today's runner constants (harness/runner.py), the starting point for tuning.
+CONFIRM = 2
+DONE_WINDOW_S = 15.0
+REGRESSION_DROP = 0.3
+FAIL_MIN_FRACTION = 0.5
+
+
+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- 1. done evidence
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class DoneWeights:
+    w_monitor: float = 1.0          # a monitor "done" look
+    w_sensor_release: float = 0.0   # gripper went from closed on an object to open near the target (/arms); new, off
+    w_goal_box: float = 0.0         # detector sees the object at its goal (in the bin, on the tower); new, off
+    w_plug_count: float = 0.0       # cable-end boxes: one fewer plug in the strip; new, off
+    keep: float = 1.0               # share of the score carried to the next look when nothing argues against it
+    reset_on_no: bool = True        # a "not done" / "failing" look sets the score to 0 (outside the done window)
+    theta: float = float(CONFIRM)   # done when the score reaches this
+    window_s: float = DONE_WINDOW_S  # a second "done" within this passes, flickers in between notwithstanding
+
+
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class Look:
+    """One check. done: the verifier's answer (True / False / None = no answer). failing: monitor status failing."""
+    t: float
+    done: bool | None
+    failing: bool = False
+    same_frames: bool = False       # the same camera images as the previous done look
+    monitor: bool = True            # from the monitor: a look with no done answer still resets the count
+    sensor_release: float = 0.0     # 0..1 features from the fast sensors
+    goal_box: float = 0.0
+    plug_count: float = 0.0
+
+
+@dataclass
+class DoneState:
+    score: float = 0.0
+    last_done_t: float | None = None
+
+
+def done_step(state: DoneState, look: Look, w: DoneWeights = DoneWeights()) -> tuple[DoneState, bool]:
+    """Folds one look into the done score. Returns (new state, stage passes now)."""
+    fast = w.w_sensor_release * look.sensor_release + w.w_goal_box * look.goal_box + w.w_plug_count * look.plug_count
+    if look.done is True and look.same_frames:
+        return DoneState(state.score * w.keep + fast, state.last_done_t), state.score * w.keep + fast >= w.theta
+    if look.done is True:
+        recent = state.last_done_t is not None and look.t - state.last_done_t <= w.window_s
+        score = state.score * w.keep + w.w_monitor + fast
+        return DoneState(score, look.t), score >= w.theta or recent
+    # The runner: a no / failing look right after a done (within the window) changes nothing; otherwise it resets the
+    # count. A monitor look with no done answer resets it even inside the window (the window still passes the next done).
+    flicker = look.done is False or look.failing
+    blank = look.done is None and look.monitor
+    in_window = state.last_done_t is not None and look.t - state.last_done_t <= w.window_s
+    if flicker and in_window:
+        score = state.score
+    elif (flicker or blank) and w.reset_on_no:
+        score = fast
+    elif flicker or blank:
+        score = state.score * w.keep + fast
+    else:
+        score = state.score * w.keep + fast
+    return DoneState(score, state.last_done_t), score >= w.theta
+
+
+def run_looks(looks: Iterable[Look], w: DoneWeights = DoneWeights()) -> float | None:
+    """Time of the look that passes the stage, or None."""
+    st = DoneState()
+    for lk in looks:
+        st, ok = done_step(st, lk, w)
+        if ok:
+            return lk.t
+    return None
+
+
+# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- 2. fail score
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class FailWeights:
+    w_fail: float = 1.0          # consecutive failing looks / confirm (monitor: only after min_fraction of the timeout)
+    w_stalled: float = 1.0       # consecutive "stalled" monitor looks / confirm (stages with a stall window)
+    w_stall: float = 1.0         # seconds since progress last rose / stall_s
+    w_time: float = 1.0          # elapsed / timeout_s
+    w_on_target: float = 0.0     # subtracted while a gripper holds the target (today: a one-off 20 s extension)
+    w_regress: float = 1.0       # in look_failing: (max progress - progress) / regress_drop >= 1 makes a failing look
+    confirm: int = CONFIRM
+    regress_drop: float = REGRESSION_DROP
+    min_fraction: float = FAIL_MIN_FRACTION
+    combine: str = "max"         # "max" = today's tree (any rule fires); "sum" = weak signals add up
+    theta: float = 1.0
+
+
+def look_failing(done: bool | None, status_failing: bool, progress: float | None, max_progress: float | None,
+                 done_seen: bool, w: FailWeights = FailWeights()) -> bool:
+    """Is one look a failing look? The verifier said no, the monitor said failing, or progress fell regress_drop
+    below the attempt's best (not counted once a done has been seen, as in the runner)."""
+    regress = 0.0
+    if progress is not None and max_progress is not None and not done_seen:
+        regress = w.w_regress * max(0.0, max_progress - progress) / w.regress_drop
+    return done is False or status_failing or regress >= 1.0 - 1e-9
+
+
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class AttemptState:
+    elapsed: float
+    timeout_s: float
+    consec_fail: int = 0                 # failing looks in a row (look_failing)
+    consec_stalled: int = 0              # "stalled" monitor looks in a row
+    since_rise_s: float | None = None    # seconds since progress last rose by STALL_RISE (None: no progress yet)
+    stall_s: float | None = None         # the stage's stall window (None: no stall rules)
+    monitor: bool = True                 # failing looks come from the monitor (then min_fraction applies)
+    on_target: bool = False
+
+
+def fail_terms(a: AttemptState, w: FailWeights = FailWeights()) -> dict[str, float]:
+    """Each term reaches its weight when its hard rule would fire on its own."""
+    gate_ok = not a.monitor or a.elapsed >= w.min_fraction * a.timeout_s
+    terms = {"fail": w.w_fail * (a.consec_fail / w.confirm) if gate_ok else 0.0,
+             "time": w.w_time * a.elapsed / a.timeout_s}
+    if a.stall_s is not None:
+        terms["stalled"] = w.w_stalled * a.consec_stalled / w.confirm
+        if a.since_rise_s is not None:
+            terms["stall"] = w.w_stall * a.since_rise_s / a.stall_s
+    return terms
+
+
+def fail_score(a: AttemptState, w: FailWeights = FailWeights()) -> float:
+    terms = list(fail_terms(a, w).values())
+    s = max(terms) if w.combine == "max" else sum(terms)
+    return s - (w.w_on_target if a.on_target else 0.0)
+
+
+def attempt_fails(a: AttemptState, w: FailWeights = FailWeights()) -> bool:
+    return fail_score(a, w) >= w.theta - 1e-9
+
+
+# -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- 3. value of the time left
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class StageModel:
+    """One stage for planning. Success by time tau: F(tau) = p_max * (1 - exp(-max(0, tau - t_min) / tau_c)).
+    `gate`: a pass bar; if it fails, nothing after it scores (it is retried until the clock instead of skipped).
+    Fit p_max, t_min, tau_c per stage from run logs or the stress sim."""
+    name: str
+    value: float
+    p_max: float
+    tau_c: float
+    t_min: float = 0.0
+    gate: bool = False
+
+    def F(self, tau: float) -> float:
+        if tau <= self.t_min:
+            return 0.0
+        return self.p_max * (1.0 - math.exp(-(tau - self.t_min) / self.tau_c))
+
+    def mean_success_time(self, T: float, step: float = 1.0) -> float:
+        """Expected time spent given success by T (integral of tau dF over [0, T] / F(T))."""
+        FT = self.F(T)
+        if FT <= 0:
+            return T
+        n = max(1, int(T / step))
+        acc, prev = 0.0, 0.0
+        for k in range(1, n + 1):
+            tau = k * T / n
+            f = self.F(tau)
+            acc += (tau - T / n / 2) * (f - prev)
+            prev = f
+        return acc / FT
+
+
+@dataclass
+class Plan:
+    """W[i][L]: expected points from stage i on with L whole seconds left; best_T[i][L]: that stage's timeout."""
+    stages: Sequence[StageModel]
+    horizon_s: int
+    W: list[list[float]] = field(default_factory=list)
+    best_T: list[list[int]] = field(default_factory=list)
+
+    def value(self, i: int, left_s: float) -> float:
+        if i >= len(self.stages):
+            return 0.0
+        return self.W[i][max(0, min(self.horizon_s, int(left_s)))]
+
+    def timeout(self, i: int, left_s: float) -> int:
+        return self.best_T[i][max(0, min(self.horizon_s, int(left_s)))]
+
+
+def plan_timeouts(stages: Sequence[StageModel], horizon_s: int, step_s: int = 1) -> Plan:
+    """Backward DP over the stage list. For a gate stage a failure leaves 0 (nothing later scores)."""
+    n = len(stages)
+    W = [[0.0] * (horizon_s + 1) for _ in range(n + 1)]
+    best = [[0] * (horizon_s + 1) for _ in range(n)]
+    for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
+        st = stages[i]
+        succ_t = [st.mean_success_time(T) for T in range(horizon_s + 1)]
+        for L in range(horizon_s + 1):
+            top, top_T = W[i + 1][L], 0           # T = 0: skip the stage
+            for T in range(step_s, L + 1, step_s):
+                p = st.F(T)
+                after_ok = W[i + 1][max(0, L - int(round(succ_t[T])))]
+                after_fail = 0.0 if st.gate else W[i + 1][L - T]
+                v = p * (st.value + after_ok) + (1 - p) * after_fail
+                if v > top + 1e-12:
+                    top, top_T = v, T
+            W[i][L], best[i][L] = top, top_T
+    return Plan(stages, horizon_s, W, best)
+
+
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class Action:
+    """A choice at a decision point: p = chance it succeeds the stage, cost_s = seconds it uses, risk = 0..1 chance of
+    something costly (a DQ, a knocked tower, an illegal ball)."""
+    name: str
+    p: float
+    cost_s: float
+    risk: float = 0.0
+
+
+def action_value(plan: Plan, i: int, left_s: float, a: Action, risk_weight: float = 50.0) -> float:
+    """Q(a) = p (V_i + W_{i+1}(L - c)) + (1 - p) W_i'(L - c) - risk_weight * risk, where after a failed action the
+    same stage can still be tried with the time left (W_i), or nothing later scores for a gate that ran out."""
+    st = plan.stages[i]
+    rest = left_s - a.cost_s
+    if rest < 0:
+        return -risk_weight * a.risk
+    ok = a.p * (st.value + plan.value(i + 1, rest))
+    again = (1 - a.p) * plan.value(i, rest)
+    return ok + again - risk_weight * a.risk
+
+
+def choose(plan: Plan, i: int, left_s: float, actions: Sequence[Action], risk_weight: float = 50.0) -> Action:
+    return max(actions, key=lambda a: action_value(plan, i, left_s, a, risk_weight))
+
+
+def with_weights(obj, **kw):
+    """Copy of a weights dataclass with some fields changed (for hill climbing)."""
+    return replace(obj, **kw)
+
+
+# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ 4. shadow mode
+def weights_from(raw: dict | None) -> tuple[DoneWeights, FailWeights]:
+    """plan `decide: {done: {...}, fail: {...}}` -> weights. Unknown keys are an error (a typo must not pass)."""
+    raw = raw or {}
+    extra = set(raw) - {"done", "fail"}
+    if extra:
+        raise ValueError(f"plan.decide: unknown key(s): {', '.join(sorted(extra))}")
+    out = []
+    for key, cls in (("done", DoneWeights), ("fail", FailWeights)):
+        part = raw.get(key) or {}
+        if not isinstance(part, dict):
+            raise ValueError(f"plan.decide.{key}: must be a mapping")
+        names = set(cls.__dataclass_fields__)
+        bad = set(part) - names
+        if bad:
+            raise ValueError(f"plan.decide.{key}: unknown weight(s): {', '.join(sorted(bad))}")
+        if key == "fail" and part.get("combine", "max") not in ("max", "sum"):
+            raise ValueError("plan.decide.fail.combine: max or sum")
+        out.append(cls(**part))
+    return out[0], out[1]
+
+
+class Shadow:
+    """Runs the equations next to the runner on every look and reports what they would decide. It never acts.
+
+    The runner calls start() at each attempt and look() once per verdict, with the inputs it already computed
+    (`failing` includes the regression rule). Counts follow the runner's: a failing look right after a done (within
+    the window) is not counted, a done resets the failing count."""
+
+    def __init__(self, done: DoneWeights = DoneWeights(), fail: FailWeights = FailWeights(), *,
+                 fixed: set[str] = frozenset()):
+        self.base_done, self.base_fail = done, fail
+        self.done_w, self.fail_w = done, fail
+        self._fixed = set(fixed)        # "done.theta", "fail.confirm": set by the plan, not taken from the stage
+        self._st = DoneState()
+        self._consec_fail = 0
+        self._timeout = 1.0
+        self._stall: float | None = None
+
+    @classmethod
+    def from_plan(cls, raw: dict | None) -> "Shadow":
+        done, fail = weights_from(raw)
+        raw = raw or {}
+        fixed = {f"{k}.{n}" for k in ("done", "fail") for n in (raw.get(k) or {})}
+        return cls(done, fail, fixed=fixed)
+
+    def start(self, timeout_s: float, stall_s: float | None, confirm: int = CONFIRM) -> None:
+        """Per attempt. The stage's confirm sets the done threshold and the failing-look count unless the plan fixed them."""
+        self.done_w = self.base_done if "done.theta" in self._fixed else replace(self.base_done, theta=float(confirm))
+        self.fail_w = self.base_fail if "fail.confirm" in self._fixed else replace(self.base_fail, confirm=int(confirm))
+        self._st, self._consec_fail = DoneState(), 0
+        self._timeout, self._stall = timeout_s, stall_s
+
+    def look(self, elapsed: float, done: bool | None, failing: bool, *, same_frames: bool, monitor: bool,
+             consec_stalled: int, since_rise_s: float | None, sensor_release: float = 0.0, goal_box: float = 0.0,
+             plug_count: float = 0.0) -> dict:
+        lk = Look(elapsed, done, failing=failing and done is not False, same_frames=same_frames, monitor=monitor,
+                  sensor_release=sensor_release, goal_box=goal_box, plug_count=plug_count)
+        in_window = self._st.last_done_t is not None and elapsed - self._st.last_done_t <= self.done_w.window_s
+        self._st, passes = done_step(self._st, lk, self.done_w)
+        if done is True and not same_frames:
+            self._consec_fail = 0
+        elif (failing or done is False) and not in_window:
+            self._consec_fail += 1
+        elif not (failing or done is False) and (done is not None or monitor):
+            self._consec_fail = 0
+        a = AttemptState(elapsed=elapsed, timeout_s=self._timeout, consec_fail=self._consec_fail,
+                         consec_stalled=consec_stalled, since_rise_s=since_rise_s, stall_s=self._stall, monitor=monitor)
+        terms = fail_terms(a, self.fail_w)
+        score = fail_score(a, self.fail_w)
+        return {"done_score": round(self._st.score, 3), "would_pass": bool(passes),
+                "fail_score": round(score, 3), "would_fail": bool(not passes and score >= self.fail_w.theta - 1e-9),
+                "terms": {k: round(v, 3) for k, v in terms.items()}}

+"""Illegal-ball guard on live camera frames, in its own thread.
+
+The plan guard otherwise looks at debug-view trips (one every ~3.4 s on the station) once per poll (check_every_s,
+3 s in v4), so a held ball could be up to ~6 s old when judged, and pick-to-bin takes a few seconds. The station
+add-on (station_addons/sitecustomize.py) serves the controller's newest frame at GET /frame/<camera>.jpg, live at
+30 fps whether or not a run is going (checked read-only on the NUC, 7 Nov: 4 ms per frame). This thread runs the
+local detector's guard on each wrist camera at `rate_hz`; `confirm_frames` yes answers in a row on new frames of the
+same camera POST /stop at once, without waiting for the runner's next poll. The runner then ends the match as for
+any guard trip.
+
+Reads only GET /frame; the one thing it sends is /stop, and only while armed (a stage the plan guard covers).
+
+Safety rules (from the Codex review of the first version):
+- The guard never waits on the runner: it sets `tripped` and sends /stop at once. The runner checks `tripped` before
+  each /start, /prompt or /home and again after it; a trip it finds afterwards may have been overtaken by its own
+  command, so it sends /stop again (Runner._motion).
+- Arming has a generation: a judgment that started before a disarm (or a re-arm for another stage) is dropped.
+- Only a new frame counts as a look: the same bytes judged again neither add a yes nor refresh `last_answer_t`, so a
+  frozen camera shows up as blindness (plan guard max_blind_s), not as "all clear".
+- Yes answers are counted per camera, so motion in one wrist view can't confirm a frozen yes in the other.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import hashlib
+import threading
+from concurrent.futures import ALL_COMPLETED, Future, ThreadPoolExecutor, wait
+import time
+import urllib.error
+import urllib.request
+from collections.abc import Callable
+from typing import Any
+
+
+class LiveFrames:
+    """GET /frame/<role>.jpg from the station API for each role, all roles at once (one slow or dead camera must not
+    hold up the others: 4 ms per frame on the NUC, so `timeout_s` is short). A role that fails this tick is missing
+    from the result and is tried again next tick (a camera that recovers is used again)."""
+
+    def __init__(self, base_url: str = "http://127.0.0.1:8091", roles: tuple[str, ...] = ("wrist_l", "wrist_r"),
+                 *, timeout_s: float = 0.25):
+        self.base_url = base_url.rstrip("/")
+        self.roles = list(roles)
+        self.timeout_s = timeout_s
+        self.last_error: str | None = None
+        self._pool = ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=max(1, len(self.roles)), thread_name_prefix="live-frame")
+        self._pending: dict[str, Future] = {}
+
+    def _one(self, role: str) -> bytes | None:
+        try:
+            with urllib.request.urlopen(f"{self.base_url}/frame/{role}.jpg", timeout=self.timeout_s) as resp:
+                return resp.read()
+        except urllib.error.HTTPError as exc:
+            self.last_error = f"{role}: HTTP {exc.code}"
+        except (urllib.error.URLError, OSError, TimeoutError) as exc:
+            self.last_error = f"{role}: {exc}"
+        return None
+
+    def grab(self, wait_s: float = 0.05) -> dict[str, bytes]:
+        """The frames that are in within `wait_s`. A camera whose fetch is still running is not waited for: its
+        fetch keeps going and its frame is returned by a later grab, so a dead camera never slows a live one."""
+        for role in self.roles:
+            if role not in self._pending:
+                self._pending[role] = self._pool.submit(self._one, role)
+        wait(list(self._pending.values()), timeout=wait_s, return_when=ALL_COMPLETED)
+        out: dict[str, bytes] = {}
+        for role, job in list(self._pending.items()):
+            if job.done():
+                del self._pending[role]
+                data = job.result()
+                if data is not None:
+                    out[role] = data
+        return out
+
+    def probe(self, tries: int = 3) -> list[str]:
+        """Roles that returned a frame in at least one of `tries` grabs (startup check before relying on them)."""
+        seen: set[str] = set()
+        for _ in range(tries):
+            seen |= set(self.grab(wait_s=self.timeout_s + 0.1))   # let each fetch finish or time out
+            if len(seen) == len(self.roles):
+                break
+        return [r for r in self.roles if r in seen]
+
+
+class LiveGuard:
+    """Runs `judge({role: jpeg})` per camera at `rate_hz` while armed. `judge` returns (True/False/None, why) like
+    TableDetector.guard. `confirm_frames` yes answers in a row on new frames of one camera call `stop()` once and
+    set `tripped` to the reason. A no on new frames of that camera resets its count; None leaves it alone."""
+
+    def __init__(self, source: Callable[[], dict[str, bytes]],
+                 judge: Callable[[dict[str, bytes]], tuple[bool | None, str]],
+                 stop: Callable[[], Any], *, rate_hz: float = 10.0, confirm_frames: int = 3,
+                 log: Callable[..., None] | None = None, clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic):
+        if confirm_frames < 1 or rate_hz <= 0:
+            raise ValueError("confirm_frames must be >= 1 and rate_hz > 0")
+        self.source, self.judge, self._stop = source, judge, stop
+        self.period = 1.0 / rate_hz
+        self.confirm_frames = confirm_frames
+        self._log = log or (lambda *a, **k: None)
+        self._clock = clock
+        self._lock = threading.Lock()           # guards the fields below
+        self._armed: str | None = None          # the covered stage's name while armed
+        self._gen = 0                           # bumped on every arm-from-disarmed and every disarm
+        self._count: dict[str, int] = {}        # per camera: yes answers in a row on new frames
+        self._sig: dict[str, str] = {}          # per camera: signature of the last frame judged
+        self.tripped: str | None = None
+        self.last_answer_t: float | None = None   # clock() of the last yes/no answer on a new frame
+        self.ticks = 0
+        self._closed = threading.Event()
+        self._thread: threading.Thread | None = None
+
+    # -- control -------------------------------------------------------------------------------
+    def arm(self, stage: str) -> None:
+        with self._lock:
+            if self._armed is None:
+                self._gen += 1
+                self._count, self._sig = {}, {}
+                self.last_answer_t = self._clock()   # max_blind_s counts from arming, as the poll guard does
+                self._log("live_guard_armed", stage=stage)
+            self._armed = stage
+
+    @property
+    def armed(self) -> bool:
+        return self._armed is not None
+
+    def disarm(self) -> None:
+        with self._lock:
+            if self._armed is not None:
+                self._log("live_guard_disarmed", stage=self._armed, ticks=self.ticks)
+            self._armed = None
+            self._gen += 1
+
+    def start(self) -> None:
+        if self._thread is None:
+            self._thread = threading.Thread(target=self._loop, name="live-guard", daemon=True)
+            self._thread.start()
+
+    def close(self) -> None:
+        self._closed.set()
+        self.disarm()
+        if self._thread is not None:
+            self._thread.join(timeout=2.0)
+
+    # -- one look ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
+    def step(self) -> None:
+        """One tick: grab, judge each camera, count, maybe stop. The thread calls this; tests call it directly."""
+        with self._lock:
+            stage, gen = self._armed, self._gen
+        if stage is None or self.tripped is not None:
+            return
+        frames = self.source()
+        self.ticks += 1
+        for role in sorted(frames):
+            jpeg = frames[role]
+            sig = hashlib.sha1(jpeg).hexdigest()
+            with self._lock:
+                if gen != self._gen:
+                    return                  # disarmed or re-armed while looking: these answers are stale
+                if sig == self._sig.get(role):
+                    continue                # the same frame again is not a new look
+            answer, why = self.judge({role: jpeg})
+            with self._lock:
+                if gen != self._gen:
+                    return
+                self._sig[role] = sig
+                if answer is None:
+                    continue
+                self.last_answer_t = self._clock()
+                if answer is False:
+                    self._count[role] = 0
+                    continue
+                self._count[role] = count = self._count.get(role, 0) + 1
+            self._log("guard_yes", stage=stage, count=count, reason=why, camera=role, source="live")
+            if count >= self.confirm_frames:
+                self._trip(stage, gen, why)
+                return
+
+    def _trip(self, stage: str, gen: int, why: str) -> None:
+        with self._lock:
+            if gen != self._gen or self.tripped is not None:
+                return
+            self.tripped = why   # set before /stop: the runner sends nothing that moves an arm from here on
+        try:
+            self._stop()
+            self._log("guard_tripped", stage=stage, reason=why, source="live", stop="sent")
+        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - the runner's own stop follows on its next check
+            self._log("guard_tripped", stage=stage, reason=why, source="live", stop=f"failed: {exc}")
+
+    def _loop(self) -> None:
+        while not self._closed.is_set():
+            t = self._clock()
+            try:
+                self.step()
+            except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - a bad tick must not kill the guard
+                self._log("live_guard_error", error=f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}")
+            self._closed.wait(max(0.0, self.period - (self._clock() - t)))
