+"""Read-only LiveKit camera observer for Hill Climb practice and evaluation."""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import argparse
+import asyncio
+import io
+import logging
+import math
+import os
+import time
+from dataclasses import asdict, dataclass
+from typing import TYPE_CHECKING
+
+from .log import RunLog
+from .plan import Stage, load_plan
+from .verifiers import MonitorVerifier
+
+if TYPE_CHECKING:
+    from livekit import rtc
+
+# Ported from codex/hill-climb-livekit (569607e) without that branch's plan changes, so the camera-name helper
+# lives here and a stage without required_cameras needs none.
+_ROLE_NAMES = {
+    "scene": "overhead", "top": "overhead", "overhead": "overhead",
+    "left": "wrist_l", "wrist_l": "wrist_l",
+    "right": "wrist_r", "wrist_r": "wrist_r",
+}
+
+
+def camera_matches(role: str, wanted: str) -> bool:
+    """Match YAM station and simulator names, or a custom camera role substring."""
+    actual = role.removeprefix("video.").removeprefix("image_window.")
+    expected = wanted.removeprefix("video.").removeprefix("image_window.")
+    canonical = _ROLE_NAMES.get(expected)
+    return wanted in role or (canonical is not None and _ROLE_NAMES.get(actual) == canonical)
+
+
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class ReceivedFrame:
+    event: rtc.VideoFrameEvent
+    received_at: float
+
+    def jpeg(self) -> bytes:
+        from PIL import Image
+
+        frame = self.event.frame
+        image = Image.frombytes("RGB", (frame.width, frame.height), bytes(frame.data))
+        # The SDK enumerates clockwise rotation in quarter turns.
+        if self.event.rotation:
+            image = image.rotate(-90 * int(self.event.rotation), expand=True)
+        buffer = io.BytesIO()
+        image.save(buffer, format="JPEG", quality=85)
+        return buffer.getvalue()
+
+
+class LiveKitCameras:
+    """Keep only the latest frame for each explicitly named camera."""
+
+    def __init__(self, publisher: str, tracks: dict[str, str]):
+        from livekit import rtc
+
+        self.room = rtc.Room()
+        self.publisher = publisher
+        self._roles = {name: role for role, name in tracks.items()}
+        self._latest: dict[str, ReceivedFrame] = {}
+        self._streams: dict[str, tuple[str, asyncio.Task]] = {}
+        self._tasks: set[asyncio.Task] = set()
+        self._reconnecting = False
+        self._closing = False
+        self.closed = False
+        self.room.on("track_published", self._subscribe)
+        self.room.on("track_subscribed", self._start_stream)
+        self.room.on("track_unsubscribed", self._end_stream)
+        self.room.on("track_muted", self._mute)
+        self.room.on("reconnecting", self._pause)
+        self.room.on("reconnected", self._resume)
+        self.room.on("disconnected", self._disconnected)
+
+    async def connect(self, url: str, token: str) -> None:
+        from livekit import rtc
+
+        await self.room.connect(url, token, rtc.RoomOptions(auto_subscribe=False, connect_timeout=10))
+        for participant in self.room.remote_participants.values():
+            for publication in participant.track_publications.values():
+                self._subscribe(publication, participant)
+
+    def _subscribe(self, publication: rtc.RemoteTrackPublication, participant: rtc.RemoteParticipant) -> None:
+        from livekit import rtc
+
+        if (participant.identity == self.publisher and publication.name in self._roles
+                and publication.kind == rtc.TrackKind.KIND_VIDEO):
+            publication.set_subscribed(True)
+
+    def _start_stream(self, track: rtc.RemoteVideoTrack, publication: rtc.RemoteTrackPublication,
+                      participant: rtc.RemoteParticipant) -> None:
+        role = self._roles.get(publication.name)
+        if participant.identity != self.publisher or role is None:
+            return
+        previous = self._streams.get(role)
+        if previous is not None:
+            sid, task = previous
+            if sid == publication.sid and not task.done():
+                return
+            task.cancel()
+        self._latest.pop(role, None)
+        task = asyncio.create_task(self._receive(role, track, publication))
+        self._streams[role] = (publication.sid, task)
+        self._tasks.add(task)
+        task.add_done_callback(self._stream_done)
+
+    def _stream_done(self, task: asyncio.Task) -> None:
+        self._tasks.discard(task)
+        if not task.cancelled() and (error := task.exception()) is not None:
+            logging.getLogger(__name__).warning("LiveKit video stream failed (%s)", type(error).__name__)
+
+    async def _receive(self, role: str, track: rtc.RemoteVideoTrack,
+                       publication: rtc.RemoteTrackPublication) -> None:
+        from livekit import rtc
+
+        stream = rtc.VideoStream.from_track(track=track, capacity=1, format=rtc.VideoBufferType.RGB24)
+        owner = (publication.sid, asyncio.current_task())
+        try:
+            async for event in stream:
+                if (self._streams.get(role) == owner and not publication.muted
+                        and not self._reconnecting and self.room.isconnected()):
+                    self._latest[role] = ReceivedFrame(event, time.monotonic())
+        finally:
+            if self._streams.get(role) == owner:
+                self._streams.pop(role)
+                self._latest.pop(role, None)
+            await stream.aclose()
+
+    def _end_stream(self, track: rtc.RemoteTrack, publication: rtc.RemoteTrackPublication,
+                    participant: rtc.RemoteParticipant) -> None:
+        role = self._roles.get(publication.name)
+        active = self._streams.get(role) if role is not None else None
+        if active is not None and active[0] == publication.sid:
+            _, task = self._streams.pop(role)
+            self._latest.pop(role, None)
+            task.cancel()
+
+    def _mute(self, participant: rtc.RemoteParticipant, publication: rtc.RemoteTrackPublication) -> None:
+        role = self._roles.get(publication.name)
+        active = self._streams.get(role) if role is not None else None
+        if active is not None and active[0] == publication.sid:
+            self._latest.pop(role, None)
+
+    def _pause(self) -> None:
+        self._reconnecting = True
+        self._latest.clear()
+
+    def _resume(self) -> None:
+        self._reconnecting = False
+
+    def _disconnected(self, reason: object) -> None:
+        self._latest.clear()
+        self.closed = not self._closing
+
+    def sample(self, now: float, max_age: float, previous: dict[str, ReceivedFrame]) -> dict[str, ReceivedFrame]:
+        """Return a complete set only when each view is fresh and new to the caller."""
+        if self._reconnecting or not self.room.isconnected() or len(self._latest) != len(self._roles):
+            return {}
+        if any(now - frame.received_at > max_age or frame is previous.get(role)
+               for role, frame in self._latest.items()):
+            return {}
+        return dict(self._latest)
+
+    async def close(self) -> None:
+        self._closing = True
+        tasks = tuple(self._tasks)
+        for task in tasks:
+            if not task.done() and not task.cancelling():
+                task.cancel()
+        await asyncio.gather(*tasks, return_exceptions=True)
+        if self.room.isconnected():
+            await self.room.disconnect()
+
+
+async def observe(publisher: str, tracks: dict[str, str], url: str, token: str, log: RunLog, *,
+                  duration: float, interval: float, max_age: float, monitor: MonitorVerifier | None,
+                  stage: Stage | None) -> tuple[int, bool]:
+    cameras = LiveKitCameras(publisher, tracks)
+    previous: dict[str, ReceivedFrame] = {}
+    samples = 0
+    try:
+        await cameras.connect(url, token)
+        started = next_sample = time.monotonic()
+        end = started + duration
+        while time.monotonic() < end and not cameras.closed:
+            now = time.monotonic()
+            current = cameras.sample(now, max_age, previous) if now >= next_sample else {}
+            if not current:
+                await asyncio.sleep(min(0.1, end - now))
+                continue
+            samples += 1
+            previous = current
+            next_sample = now + interval
+            frames = {role: frame.jpeg() for role, frame in current.items()}
+            files = log.save_frames("livekit", frames)
+            log.event("livekit_sample", sample=samples, frames=[file.name for file in files],
+                      cameras={role: {"rtc_timestamp_us": frame.event.timestamp_us,
+                                      "receive_age_ms": round(1000 * (now - frame.received_at), 1)}
+                               for role, frame in current.items()})
+            if monitor is not None and stage is not None:
+                request_started = time.monotonic()
+                verdict = await asyncio.to_thread(monitor.check_now, stage, frames, now - started)
+                log.event("livekit_verdict", sample=samples, stage=stage.name,
+                          request_ms=round(1000 * (time.monotonic() - request_started), 1), **asdict(verdict))
+        return samples, cameras.closed
+    finally:
+        await cameras.close()
+
+
+def positive_seconds(value: str) -> float:
+    number = float(value)
+    if not math.isfinite(number) or number <= 0:
+        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError("expected a finite positive number")
+    return number
+
+
+def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
+    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
+    parser.add_argument("--publisher", required=True, help="LiveKit participant identity that owns the cameras")
+    parser.add_argument("--track", action="append", required=True, metavar="ROLE=NAME",
+                        help="published video track name for overhead, wrist_l or wrist_r")
+    parser.add_argument("--seconds", type=positive_seconds, default=30, help="observation window after connecting")
+    parser.add_argument("--interval", type=positive_seconds, default=3, help="minimum seconds between samples")
+    parser.add_argument("--max-age", type=positive_seconds, default=1, help="max seconds since local frame receipt")
+    parser.add_argument("--runs-dir", default="runs_livekit")
+    parser.add_argument("--plan", help="optional harness YAML for an advisory verdict")
+    parser.add_argument("--stage", help="stage to assess in the observer")
+    parser.add_argument("--var", action="append", default=[], metavar="NAME=VALUE")
+    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
+    tracks = {}
+    for item in args.track:
+        role, _, name = item.partition("=")
+        if role not in ("overhead", "wrist_l", "wrist_r") or not name or role in tracks or name in tracks.values():
+            parser.error("--track needs a unique overhead, wrist_l or wrist_r role and a unique track name")
+        tracks[role] = name
+    if bool(args.plan) != bool(args.stage):
+        parser.error("--plan and --stage must be used together")
+    url, token = os.getenv("LIVEKIT_URL"), os.getenv("LIVEKIT_TOKEN")
+    if not url or not token:
+        parser.error("set LIVEKIT_URL and LIVEKIT_TOKEN")
+    try:
+        from livekit import rtc
+        from PIL import Image
+    except ImportError:
+        parser.error("install livekit and pillow to use the observer")
+    monitor, stage = None, None
+    if args.plan:
+        variables = {}
+        for item in args.var:
+            name, _, value = item.partition("=")
+            if not name or not value:
+                parser.error("--var expects NAME=VALUE")
+            variables[name] = value
+        try:
+            plan = load_plan(args.plan, variables)
+            stage = next((stage for stage in plan.stages if stage.name == args.stage), None)
+            if stage is None:
+                parser.error(f"unknown stage {args.stage!r}")
+            missing = [role for role in getattr(stage, "required_cameras", []) if not any(camera_matches(r, role) for r in tracks)]
+            if missing:
+                parser.error(f"stage requires camera roles {', '.join(missing)}")
+            monitor = MonitorVerifier(plan, history_frames=2, timeout_s=5)
+        except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:
+            parser.error(str(exc))
+    log = RunLog(args.runs_dir)
+    log.update_manifest(source="livekit", publisher=args.publisher, tracks=tracks, stage=args.stage)
+    try:
+        samples, disconnected = asyncio.run(observe(args.publisher, tracks, url, token, log, duration=args.seconds,
+                                                    interval=args.interval, max_age=args.max_age,
+                                                    monitor=monitor, stage=stage))
+    except KeyboardInterrupt:
+        print(f"Observer stopped. Logs at {log.path}")
+        return 130
+    except (rtc.ConnectError, TimeoutError) as exc:
+        log.event("livekit_connect_failed", error_type=type(exc).__name__)
+        print(f"LiveKit connection failed. Logs at {log.path}")
+        return 1
+    log.write_summary({"samples": samples, "disconnected": disconnected})
+    print(f"Saved {samples} LiveKit camera sets to {log.path}")
+    return 0 if samples and not disconnected else 1
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    raise SystemExit(main())

+"""The operator talks to the harness in plain words while it runs (testing and tuning only: any human input during a
+match is a DQ).
+
+Each typed line goes to Claude with the plan and the run so far. Claude answers it and says what to do about it:
+end the current stage, skip later stages, send the robot a new instruction now, or keep a lasting note ("next time
+approach the tower from the left") that the monitor follows for the rest of the run and that is saved as a lesson
+for later runs. The runner applies the actions between checks; everything goes to events.jsonl.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import base64
+import json
+import threading
+from collections.abc import Callable
+from dataclasses import dataclass, field
+from pathlib import Path
+from typing import Any
+
+from .plan import Plan
+from .verifiers import valid_jpeg
+
+MAX_CONTEXT_EVENTS = 40
+SHOWN = ("stage_start", "prompt_change", "prompt_rewrite", "verdict", "attempt_end", "stage_skipped", "recovery",
+         "retry", "auto_reset", "station_restart", "guard_yes", "operator_input", "operator_reply", "tower_prompt")
+
+INSTRUCTION = """You are the operator's assistant inside a robot test harness. The operator is watching the robot and
+typed the message below. Read it as plain speech: it may be a question ("why did it pick the pink block?"), an order
+("skip the next step", "stop stacking, go to the balls"), or feedback on how to do things ("grip the plug lower next
+time"). Reply to the operator in 1-3 short sentences, then output JSON only between <json> and </json>:
+{"reply": str,
+ "end_current_stage": bool,          // true: give up the current stage now and go on with the next one
+ "skip_stages": [str],                // names of LATER stages to skip (exact names from the stage list)
+ "prompt_now": str|null,              // a new instruction for the robot to follow right now, max 200 characters
+ "note": str|null}                    // lasting feedback for how prompts and judging should go from now on
+This is a test run to tune the harness, not a match: do what the operator asks, even where a match rule would
+forbid it, and never argue. Only set an action the operator asked for. A question alone gets only a reply. When the operator wants to skip or
+move past what the robot is doing now, set end_current_stage true (skip_stages only lists LATER stages); when they
+ask for another task, also skip the stages before it. Set
+"note" only when the operator says how the robot or the judging should do things (it is kept for later runs), never
+for a skip, a stop or another one-off order; word it as their instruction."""
+
+
+@dataclass
+class Actions:
+    end_current_stage: bool = False
+    skip_stages: set[str] = field(default_factory=set)
+    prompts: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
+    notes: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
+
+
+def parse_reply(text: str, stage_names: list[str]) -> tuple[str, dict[str, Any]]:
+    start, end = text.rfind("<json>"), text.rfind("</json>")
+    if start < 0 or end < start:
+        return text.strip(), {}
+    try:
+        obj = json.loads(text[start + len("<json>"):end])
+    except ValueError:
+        return text[:start].strip(), {}
+    if not isinstance(obj, dict):
+        return text[:start].strip(), {}
+    out: dict[str, Any] = {}
+    if obj.get("end_current_stage") is True:
+        out["end_current_stage"] = True
+    skips = [s for s in obj.get("skip_stages") or [] if isinstance(s, str) and s in stage_names]
+    if skips:
+        out["skip_stages"] = skips
+    for key, limit in (("prompt_now", 300), ("note", 500)):
+        v = obj.get(key)
+        if isinstance(v, str) and v.strip():
+            out[key] = " ".join(v.split())[:limit]
+    reply = obj.get("reply") if isinstance(obj.get("reply"), str) else text[:start]
+    return reply.strip(), out
+
+
+class Operator:
+    """Collects the operator's lines, asks Claude about each in a background thread, queues the actions."""
+
+    def __init__(self, plan: Plan, log: Any, *, transport: Callable[[dict[str, Any]], dict[str, Any]] | None,
+                 model: str = "claude-haiku-5-5", lessons_path: Path | None = None,
+                 print_fn: Callable[[str], None] = print):
+        self.plan, self.log, self._transport, self.model = plan, log, transport, model
+        self.lessons_path = lessons_path
+        self._print = print_fn
+        self._lock = threading.Lock()
+        self._actions = Actions()
+        self._events: list[str] = []
+        self.status: Callable[[], str] = lambda: ""   # the runner's one-line state (stage, prompt, elapsed)
+        self.frames: Callable[[], dict[str, bytes]] = dict
+
+    # -- what the runner tells it --------------------------------------------------------------
+    def record(self, event: str, fields: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
+        """A condensed copy of the run's events, the context for the operator's questions."""
+        if event not in SHOWN:
+            return
+        keep = {k: v for k, v in fields.items() if k in ("stage", "prompt", "why", "reason", "done", "progress",
+                                                           "status", "holding", "ok", "error", "text", "next")}
+        with self._lock:
+            self._events = (self._events + [f"{event} {json.dumps(keep, default=str)}"])[-MAX_CONTEXT_EVENTS:]
+
+    def take(self) -> Actions:
+        with self._lock:
+            out, self._actions = self._actions, Actions()
+        return out
+
+    # -- the operator's lines ------------------------------------------------------------------
+    def submit(self, line: str) -> None:
+        self.log.event("operator_input", text=line)
+        self.record("operator_input", {"text": line})
+        if self._transport is None:
+            self._print("[operator] logged (no Claude key, so nothing is done with it)")
+            return
+        threading.Thread(target=self._handle, args=(line,), name="operator", daemon=True).start()
+
+    def _handle(self, line: str) -> None:
+        names = [s.name for s in self.plan.stages]
+        with self._lock:
+            context = "\n".join(self._events)
+        stages = "\n".join(f"  {s.name}: {s.prompt}" for s in self.plan.stages)
+        text = (f"Task: {self.plan.task}\nStages in order:\n{stages}\nNow: {self.status()}\n"
+                f"Recent events, oldest first:\n{context}\n\n{INSTRUCTION}\n\nOperator: {line}")
+        content: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
+        for role, jpeg in sorted(self.frames().items()):
+            if valid_jpeg(jpeg):
+                content += [{"type": "text", "text": f"newest {role}"},
+                            {"type": "image", "source": {"type": "base64", "media_type": "image/jpeg",
+                                                         "data": base64.b64encode(jpeg).decode("ascii")}}]
+        content.append({"type": "text", "text": text})
+        payload: dict[str, Any] = {"model": self.model, "max_tokens": 2000, "output_config": {"effort": "low"},
+                                   "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": content}]}
+        if self.plan.system_prompt:
+            payload["system"] = self.plan.system_prompt
+        try:
+            reply = self._transport(payload)
+            raw = "".join(b.get("text", "") for b in reply.get("content", []) if b.get("type") == "text")
+        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - the operator's question must never end a run
+            self.log.event("operator_reply", text=line, reply=f"error: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}")
+            self._print(f"[operator] no answer: {type(exc).__name__}")
+            return
+        answer, acts = parse_reply(raw, names)
+        self.log.event("operator_reply", text=line, reply=answer, actions=acts)
+        self.record("operator_reply", {"text": answer})
+        self._print(f"[claude] {answer}" + (f"  -> {acts}" if acts else ""))
+        with self._lock:
+            a = self._actions
+            a.end_current_stage = a.end_current_stage or bool(acts.get("end_current_stage"))
+            a.skip_stages |= set(acts.get("skip_stages", []))
+            if "prompt_now" in acts:
+                a.prompts.append(acts["prompt_now"])
+            if "note" in acts:
+                a.notes.append(acts["note"])
+        if "note" in acts:
+            self._save_lesson(acts["note"], line)
+
+    def _save_lesson(self, note: str, said: str) -> None:
+        if self.lessons_path is None:
+            return
+        try:
+            self.lessons_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+            with self.lessons_path.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
+                fh.write(f"- {note}  (operator: \"{said}\", run {self.log.path.name})\n")
+        except OSError as exc:
+            self.log.event("warning", message=f"lesson not saved: {exc}")
+
+
+def load_lessons(path: Path | None) -> str:
+    """Earlier operator lessons, for the monitor's system prompt. '' when there are none."""
+    if path is None or not path.exists():
+        return ""
+    lines = [l for l in path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines() if l.startswith("- ")]
+    return "\n".join(lines[-20:])

     def note_for(self, role: str) -> str:

+_START_POSE_KEYS = {"reference", "tolerance_rad", "home_before", "home_pose", "max_homes"}
+
+
+@dataclass
+class StartPose:
+    """Compare the arms' joints with the starts in the teleop data before a stage's prompt (harness.start_pose).
+    Every check is logged. On stages matching `home_before` (name prefixes), a start farther than `tolerance_rad`
+    from every reference start sends the arms home first, if `home_pose` is itself a reference start for that
+    prompt, at most `max_homes` times per stage. Only for stages where a homing path can't knock anything over."""
+
+    reference: str
+    tolerance_rad: float = 0.35
+    home_before: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
+    home_pose: dict[str, list[float]] = field(default_factory=lambda: {"left": [0.0] * 6, "right": [0.0] * 6})
+    max_homes: int = 1
+
+    def homes(self, stage_name: str) -> bool:
+        return any(stage_name.startswith(p) for p in self.home_before)
+
+
 @dataclass
 class Plan:
     task: str
@@ -126,8 +147,21 @@ class Plan:
     tower_target: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
     # Optional: name the next piece in the prompt instead of the stage's generic one. Shape word -> template with
     # <piece>, e.g. {"cube": "put the <piece> on top of the stack"}. The piece is the target's next entry after the
-    # tower the monitor last reported. Off (empty) for a fine-tune that only knows its recorded texts.
+    # tower the monitor last reported; an optional <top> names the piece it goes on. Off (empty) for a fine-tune
+    # that only knows its recorded texts.
     tower_prompts: dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)
+    start_pose: StartPose | None = None
+    # Optional weights for harness.decide's shadow run ({done: {...}, fail: {...}}); None = today's rules as weights.
+    # The shadow only logs `decide` events next to the runner's own calls; it never decides anything.
+    decide: dict[str, Any] | None = None

+def _decide(raw: Any) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
+    if raw is None:
+        return None
+    if not isinstance(raw, dict):
+        raise ValueError("plan.decide: must be a mapping")
+    from .decide import weights_from  # noqa: PLC0415
+    weights_from(raw)                 # validate now: a typo in a weight name fails at load, not mid-match
+    return raw
+
+
+def _start_pose(raw: Any, names: list[str]) -> StartPose | None:
+    if raw is None:
+        return None
+    if not isinstance(raw, dict):
+        raise ValueError("plan.start_pose: must be a mapping")
+    extra = set(raw) - _START_POSE_KEYS
+    if extra:
+        raise ValueError(f"plan.start_pose: unknown key(s): {', '.join(sorted(extra))}")
+    ref = _text(raw.get("reference"), "plan.start_pose.reference")
+    home_before = raw.get("home_before") or []
+    if not isinstance(home_before, list) or not all(isinstance(x, str) and x.strip() for x in home_before):
+        raise ValueError("plan.start_pose.home_before: must be a list of stage-name prefixes")
+    for prefix in home_before:
+        if not any(n.startswith(prefix) for n in names):
+            raise ValueError(f"plan.start_pose.home_before: {prefix!r} matches no stage name")
+    home = raw.get("home_pose")
+    if home is None:
+        home = {"left": [0.0] * 6, "right": [0.0] * 6}
+    if not isinstance(home, dict) or set(home) != {"left", "right"} or not all(
+            isinstance(v, list) and len(v) == 6 and all(isinstance(x, (int, float)) and not isinstance(x, bool) for x in v)
+            for v in home.values()):
+        raise ValueError("plan.start_pose.home_pose: must map left and right to 6 joint angles (rad)")
+    return StartPose(reference=ref, tolerance_rad=float(_num(raw.get("tolerance_rad", 0.35), "plan.start_pose.tolerance_rad")),
+                     home_before=[x.strip() for x in home_before],
+                     home_pose={k: [float(x) for x in v] for k, v in home.items()},
+                     max_homes=int(_num(raw.get("max_homes", 1), "plan.start_pose.max_homes", integer=True)))
+

+    def _apply_operator(self) -> tuple[bool, list[str]]:
+        """Takes the operator's queued actions: notes go to the monitor, skips are kept. Returns (end the current
+        stage, prompts to send now)."""
+        if self.operator is None:
+            return False, []
+        acts = self.operator.take()
+        if acts.skip_stages:
+            self._skip |= acts.skip_stages
+            self.log.event("operator_action", action="skip_stages", stages=sorted(acts.skip_stages))
+        monitors = {id(m): m for v in self.verifiers.values() for m in (v, getattr(v, "second", None))
+                    if hasattr(m, "operator_notes")}
+        for note in acts.notes:
+            self.log.event("operator_action", action="note", note=note)
+            for m in monitors.values():
+                m.operator_notes.append(note)
+        if acts.end_current_stage:
+            self.log.event("operator_action", action="end_current_stage", stage=self._stage_name)
+        return acts.end_current_stage, acts.prompts

+    def _shadow_look(self, stage: Stage, attempt: int, elapsed: float, verdict: Verdict, failing: bool, monitor: bool,
+                     consec_stalled: int, last_rise: float | None, *, same: bool) -> None:

+        if self.shadow is None:

+    def _start_pose_gate(self, stage: Stage, prompt: str, why: str) -> bool:
+        """Logs how far the arms are from the training starts for `prompt`. On a home_before stage with a far start,
+        stops (if running) and homes the arms. Returns True if it homed, so the caller must /start afresh."""
+        cfg = self.plan.start_pose
+        if cfg is None or self.start_poses is None:
+            return False
+        st = self.station.status()
+        joints, source = self._joint_state(st)
+        chk = self.start_poses.check(prompt, joints, cfg.tolerance_rad, source)
+        self.log.event("start_pose", stage=stage.name, why=why, prompt=prompt, tolerance_rad=cfg.tolerance_rad,
+                       **chk.as_event())
+        if chk.status != "far" or not cfg.homes(stage.name):
+            return False
+        if self._homes.get(stage.name, 0) >= cfg.max_homes:
+            self.log.event("start_pose_home", stage=stage.name, done=False, reason=f"max_homes {cfg.max_homes} used")
+            return False
+        home = self.start_poses.check(prompt, cfg.home_pose, cfg.tolerance_rad, "home_pose")
+        if home.status != "ok":
+            self.log.event("start_pose_home", stage=stage.name, done=False,
+                           reason="the home pose is not a training start for this prompt either", **home.as_event())
+            return False
+        left = self.plan.time_budget_s - (self._clock() - self._t0)
+        if left < HOME_RESERVE_S:
+            self.log.event("start_pose_home", stage=stage.name, done=False,
+                           reason=f"only {left:.0f} s left; /home can't be cancelled once started")
+            return False
+        self._check_global()
+        self._homes[stage.name] = self._homes.get(stage.name, 0) + 1
+        self.log.event("start_pose_home", stage=stage.name, done=True, why=why)
+        if st.get("state") != "idle":
+            self.station.stop()
+            self._wait({"idle"}, IDLE_TIMEOUT_S, "idle after stop")
+        self._check_global()   # an operator stop, the deadline or a guard trip during the stop: hold, don't home
+        if self.plan.time_budget_s - (self._clock() - self._t0) < HOME_RESERVE_S:
+            raise _Fail("not enough match time left to home safely after the stop")
+        self._motion(self.station.home)
+        self._wait({"idle"}, IDLE_TIMEOUT_S, "idle after home")
+        return True

@@ -0,0 +1,98 @@
+"""Is the robot starting a stage from a pose the policy was trained to start from?
+
+VLAs lose more to an unfamiliar start pose than to anything else measured in LIBERO-Plus (arXiv 2510.13626,
+OpenVLA-OFT: robot initial state -59.9 points, camera -37.4, language -15.6). A fine-tune only saw the starts in
+its teleop episodes. The reference (tools/start_poses.py) is the first frame of every task segment in those
+episodes, keyed by the exact task text, which v4 prompts match byte for byte.
+
+Joint state comes from GET /arms `positions_rad` (station add-on; works while idle) or else from the newest
+debug-view trip's observation `state.left_arm` / `state.right_arm` (only while a run is going).
+Grippers are not compared: open or closed both occur at every start in the data.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import json
+from dataclasses import dataclass
+from pathlib import Path
+from typing import Any
+
+SIDES = ("left", "right")
+
+
+@dataclass
+class PoseCheck:
+    status: str                    # ok | far | no_reference | no_state
+    distance: dict[str, float]     # per arm: max |joint - reference| (rad) to the nearest reference start
+    nearest: str | None            # the reference episode it was nearest to
+    source: str | None             # where the joint state came from
+    joints: dict[str, list[float]] | None
+
+    def as_event(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
+        return {"status": self.status, "distance_rad": {k: round(v, 3) for k, v in self.distance.items()},
+                "nearest": self.nearest, "source": self.source,
+                "joints": {k: [round(x, 3) for x in v] for k, v in (self.joints or {}).items()}}
+
+
+class StartPoses:
+    """prompt -> list of {"left": [6 rad], "right": [6 rad], "episode": "dataset/episode/segment"}."""
+
+    def __init__(self, by_prompt: dict[str, list[dict[str, Any]]]):
+        self.by_prompt = by_prompt
+
+    @classmethod
+    def load(cls, path: str | Path) -> StartPoses:
+        data = json.loads(Path(path).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
+        by = data.get("by_prompt") if isinstance(data, dict) else None
+        if not isinstance(by, dict):
+            raise ValueError(f"{path}: expected {{'by_prompt': {{prompt: [start, ...]}}}}")
+        return cls(by)
+
+    def nearest(self, prompt: str, joints: dict[str, list[float]]) -> tuple[dict[str, float], str | None]:
+        """Per-arm distance to the reference start that is nearest overall (the larger of the two arm distances)."""
+        best: tuple[float, dict[str, float], str | None] | None = None
+        for ref in self.by_prompt.get(prompt, []):
+            d = {s: _linf(joints[s], ref[s]) for s in SIDES if s in joints and s in ref}
+            if not d:
+                continue
+            worst = max(d.values())
+            if best is None or worst < best[0]:
+                best = (worst, d, ref.get("episode"))
+        return (best[1], best[2]) if best else ({}, None)
+
+    def check(self, prompt: str, joints: dict[str, list[float]] | None, tolerance_rad: float,
+              source: str | None = None) -> PoseCheck:
+        if prompt not in self.by_prompt:
+            return PoseCheck("no_reference", {}, None, source, joints)
+        if not joints:
+            return PoseCheck("no_state", {}, None, source, joints)
+        dist, ep = self.nearest(prompt, joints)
+        if not dist:
+            return PoseCheck("no_state", {}, None, source, joints)
+        status = "ok" if max(dist.values()) <= tolerance_rad else "far"
+        return PoseCheck(status, dist, ep, source, joints)
+
+
+def _linf(a: list[float], b: list[float]) -> float:
+    n = min(len(a), len(b))
+    return max((abs(float(a[i]) - float(b[i])) for i in range(n)), default=0.0)
+
+
+def joints_from_arms(arms: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, list[float]] | None:
+    """{side: [rad]} from GET /arms when the add-on reports positions_rad."""
+    out = {}
+    for side in SIDES:
+        q = (arms or {}).get(side, {}).get("positions_rad") if isinstance((arms or {}).get(side), dict) else None
+        if isinstance(q, list) and q:
+            out[side] = [float(x) for x in q]
+    return out or None
+
+
+def joints_from_observation(obs: dict[str, Any] | None) -> dict[str, list[float]] | None:
+    """{side: [rad]} from a debug-view trip observation (`state.left_arm`, `state.right_arm`)."""
+    out = {}
+    for side in SIDES:
+        q = (obs or {}).get(f"state.{side}_arm")
+        if isinstance(q, list) and q and all(isinstance(x, (int, float)) for x in q):
+            out[side] = [float(x) for x in q]
+    return out or None

 def valid_jpeg(data: bytes) -> bool:
@@ -48,6 +51,50 @@ def valid_jpeg(data: bytes) -> bool:
     return isinstance(data, (bytes, bytearray)) and len(data) > 4 and data[:2] == b"\xff\xd8" and b"\xff\xd9" in data[-32:]
 
 
+STITCH_TILE_W = 384     # 4 tiles a row: 1536 px wide, under the API's 1568 px long edge, so nothing is rescaled
+STITCH_COLS = 4
+
+
+def stitch(images: list[tuple[str, bytes]]) -> bytes | None:
+    """All the check's frames as one labelled grid JPEG (tile n shows "n. label"), in order. One mid-size image costs
+    the model far fewer tokens than ~10 separate ones, so the answer comes sooner. None when Pillow can't decode."""
+    try:
+        import io
+
+        from PIL import Image, ImageDraw, ImageFont
+    except ImportError:
+        return None
+    tiles = []
+    for label, jpeg in images:
+        try:
+            im = Image.open(io.BytesIO(jpeg)).convert("RGB")
+        except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - a bad frame is left out, not fatal
+            continue
+        h = max(1, round(im.height * STITCH_TILE_W / im.width))
+        tiles.append((label, im.resize((STITCH_TILE_W, h))))
+    if not tiles:
+        return None
+    th = max(t.height for _, t in tiles)
+    rows = (len(tiles) + STITCH_COLS - 1) // STITCH_COLS
+    cols = min(STITCH_COLS, len(tiles))
+    sheet = Image.new("RGB", (cols * STITCH_TILE_W + (cols - 1) * 4, rows * th + (rows - 1) * 4), "white")
+    draw = ImageDraw.Draw(sheet)
+    try:
+        font = ImageFont.load_default(size=15)
+    except TypeError:
+        font = ImageFont.load_default()
+    for i, (label, im) in enumerate(tiles):
+        x, y = (i % STITCH_COLS) * (STITCH_TILE_W + 4), (i // STITCH_COLS) * (th + 4)
+        sheet.paste(im, (x, y))
+        text = f"{i + 1}. {label}"
+        box = draw.textbbox((x + 4, y + 3), text, font=font)
+        draw.rectangle((box[0] - 3, box[1] - 2, box[2] + 3, box[3] + 2), fill="black")
+        draw.text((x + 4, y + 3), text, fill="white", font=font)
+    out = io.BytesIO()
+    sheet.save(out, "JPEG", quality=85)
+    return out.getvalue()
+
+
 def frames_digest(frames: dict[str, bytes]) -> str:
     h = hashlib.sha1()
     for role, jpeg in sorted(frames.items()):

+# plan.rewrite_prompts: the monitor's diagnosis becomes the robot's next instruction. Base Dream-1 follows a change of
+# object or step well and ignores fine adjustments ("1 cm higher"), so it is asked for a whole short instruction.
+NEXT_PROMPT_RULE = (
+    'next_prompt: only with status "stalled" or "failing", when the robot is on the wrong object or wrong step, '
+    'plainly stuck, or about to fail (e.g. its arm is about to hit the tower), write ONE short instruction for the '
+    'robot that replaces the last prompt: max 200 characters, plain words, one action, naming objects by colour and '
+    'position, e.g. "pick up the yellow block and place it gently on top of the pink block". It must ask for something '
+    'different from the last prompt (another object, another step), never the same request reworded: every new prompt '
+    'makes the robot start its motion over. Follow the system rules and any operator feedback. Otherwise null.')
+NEXT_PROMPT_MAX_CHARS = 300

+# Hybrid Hill Climb plan for ZERO-SHOT base Dream-1 (hillclimb_rubric_v4, 7-8 Nov 2026).
+#
+# Same match structure, pass bars, guard, tower target and 295 s clock as hill_climb_v4.yaml. What differs:
+# - Specific prompts. Base Dream-1 follows short instructions that name objects by colour and position ("pick up the
+#   pink cube ... onto the blue cube") far better than the generic task texts, which are written for a fine-tune.
+# - The monitor writes the robot's next prompt (rewrite_prompts): when it sees the robot on the wrong object or step,
+#   stuck, or about to knock the tower, its diagnosis becomes the next instruction (cooldown 15 s, max 3 an attempt;
+#   runner.REWRITE_COOLDOWN_S / REWRITE_MAX). The station lessons it follows are in system_prompt below.
+# - reset_first: /reset before the first stage, so both grippers start open (a run that started shut grasped nothing).
+# - Longer stall windows on the stacking stages (20 s cut attempts short while Dream was still lining up).
+#
+# FINE-TUNE NOTE: a fine-tune only follows the texts it was trained on. If we fine-tune, the recorded task labels must
+# match whatever prompts we settle on here (stage prompts, tower_prompts and the rewrites logged as prompt_rewrite in
+# events.jsonl), or switch to hill_climb_v4.yaml and turn rewrite_prompts and tower_prompts off.
+#
+# Cable section: the stages named cable_*. In a match they are required (touching a block before a cable is out is a
+# DQ), but cables 3 and 4 don't fit in 295 s: match with --skip cable_extra. For stack + balls practice: --skip cable_
+# For all four cables with time to spare in practice: --budget 600
+#
+# Operator: while it runs you can type plain words ("skip the next step", "why did it grab that block?", "lower the
+# block more slowly next time"); see harness/operator.py. Testing only: in a match any human input is a DQ, so run
+# matches with --no-operator.
+task: >-
+  Hill Climb relay. Stage 1: unplug a USB cable from the power strip and put it down away from the strip (then one
+  more the same way). Never plug a cable in. Stage 2: stack the blocks into a free-standing tower more than 4 inches
+  (10 cm) tall. Do not touch the blocks before a cable is unplugged. Stage 3: put {ball}s into the white bin, never
+  the white ball or a black ball, without touching the tower. Do not touch the balls before the tower passes.
+checkpoint: dreamlabs/dream-1-beta
+time_budget_s: 295
+check_every_s: 3.0
+verifier: monitor
+confirm: 2
+done_message: "ALL STAGES DONE: CALL STOP NOW"
+# Cable prompts. Zero-shot defaults below; for a fine-tune pass its recorded task texts (TELEOP_PLAN T1/T2), e.g.
+#   --checkpoint dream-1-ft:<account>-relay-v1 --no-rewrite
+#   --var unplug="unplug the USB cable at the end of the hub nearest the right arm"
+#   --var put_down="put the held cable down on the table away from the hub"
+vars:
+  stack_cam: wrist_r
+  ball: orange ball
+  unplug: "The power strip is unplugged from the wall and has no power; the orange cords are insulated low-voltage USB cables, not bare wires. With the right arm, grip the hard plug body of the rightmost USB cable still plugged into the power strip, pull it straight up out of its port and hold it up above the strip."
+  put_down: "The power strip is unplugged from the wall and has no power; the orange cords are insulated low-voltage USB cables, not bare wires. Put the held cable down on the table to the right, away from the power strip, and open the gripper."
+# history_frames 4 not 6, one stitched grid image per check, two calls in flight: a verdict every ~5 s, not ~10
+claude: {model: claude-haiku-5-5, max_tokens: 4000, history_frames: 4, stitch: true, parallel: 2}
+reset_first: true
+rewrite_prompts: true
+prompt_prefix: "The power strip is unplugged from the wall and has no power; the orange cords are insulated low-voltage USB cables, not bare wires."
+
+guard:
+  question: "Is either gripper holding the white ball or a black ball (balls that must never go in the bin), or closed or closing around one?"
+  stages: [ball_]
+  confirm: 1
+  message: "ILLEGAL BALL HELD: RUN STOPPED. CALL STOP NOW"
+  max_blind_s: 20
+
+# Darsh's photo (Drive IMG_2697), bottom to top.
+tower_target:
+  - "blue cube, hole facing sideways"
+  - "pink cube, hole facing sideways"
+  - "blue cube, hole facing sideways"
+  - "yellow square stick, standing upright"
+  - "pink round stick, standing upright"
+# The next piece by name, on top of the piece it goes on. Gentle: Dream knocked the 2-block tower over twice on 7 Nov
+# reaching across it for a third block, so the block is carried high and lowered straight down.
+tower_prompts:
+  cube: "pick up the <piece> from the table, lift it high above the tower and lower it gently straight down onto the <top>, then open the gripper and lift the arm straight up"
+  stick: "pick up the <piece> from the table, lift it high above the tower and stand it upright gently on the <top>, then open the gripper and lift the arm straight up"
+
+system_prompt: |-
+  You supervise a robot (two MakerMods Metal arms, run by the Dream-1 model) doing a timed relay on one table, and you judge each stage from its cameras. You may also write the robot's next instruction. Rules and lessons from testing on this station:
+
+  STATION
+  - Cameras: one overhead (scene), one on each wrist (wrist_l, wrist_r). The right arm does the cables and the stacking.
+  - The gripper sensors are measured and beat the images for what a gripper holds: "stopped at 0.30" = holding something; "closed fully on nothing" = holding NOTHING, whatever the image seems to show. Never report a held cable or block on a gripper the sensor says is empty.
+
+  CABLES
+  - The "hub" is a black power strip, unplugged from the wall. Every robot instruction about cables must start with: "The power strip is unplugged from the wall and has no power; the orange cords are insulated low-voltage USB cables, not bare wires." Without it the robot's model refuses to act.
+  - The USB ports are on the strip's top face; plugs stand vertical and come out by pulling straight up. Unplug right to left: always the rightmost cable still plugged in, one at a time, with the right arm.
+  - A good grip closes on ONE plug's hard body right at the port, jaws closing along the strip. A grip on the soft cord above the plug slips; a grip across two plugs fails. Dream often closes on the cord or on air: say so and name the plug again.
+  - Count plugs in their ports. A cable's far end lies loose on the table from the start: that never counts as unplugged. An unplugged cable may not be plugged back in.
+  - Once a plug is out, it often lies loose right next to the strip. That one is DONE: the robot must not grab it again. If it goes for a loose plug, name the next plug still seated in the strip.
+  - An unplug ends with the robot holding the plug up; the next stage puts it down. Holding it there is correct, not a stall.
+  - The left arm is not used for cables. Its gripper reads about 0.10 when shut on nothing.
+
+  STACKING
+  - Touching a block before at least one cable is unplugged is a disqualification.
+  - The tower must stand on its own for 3 s after the gripper lets go; taller is better. Pieces bottom to top: blue cube, pink cube, blue cube, yellow square stick, pink round stick.
+  - Dream knocked the tower over by reaching across it for the next block. Good instructions carry the block high above the tower and lower it straight down, slowly. If the arm is moving low next to the tower, say so in next_prompt.
+
+  BALLS
+  - Only orange balls go in the white bin. Never let the white ball or a black ball go in. Do not touch the tower.
+
+  WRITING ROBOT INSTRUCTIONS (next_prompt)
+  - One short action in plain words, naming objects by colour and position: "pick up the yellow block and place it gently on top of the pink block", "grip the plug body of the rightmost cable still in the strip and pull it straight up".
+  - The robot follows a change of object or step well but ignores fine adjustments like "1 cm higher". Only write next_prompt when it is on the wrong object or step, plainly stuck, or about to fail.
+
+stages:
+  # ---- Stage 1 (pass bar: one cable out). Drop with --skip cable_ for practice. ----
+  - name: cable_unplug
+    prompt: "{unplug}"

+    expect: "The right gripper closes on one plug's hard body at the port, jaws along the strip, and pulls it straight up until it is clear."
+    camera_notes:

+    timeout_s: 60
+    max_attempts: 99    # pass bar: keep trying until the clock runs out

+    success: "Is one block resting on top of another block, standing on its own, with no gripper touching either?"

+# Hill Climb plan in Dream-1's own prompt style ("long" variant of hybrid_v4.yaml), for zero-shot base Dream-1.
+#
+# Why (8 Nov, after three live runs of hybrid_v4): Dream Labs' examples for Dream-1 are short imperatives that each
+# carry a whole task: "Stack the cubes into a single tower", "Pick up all markers one by one and put into black pencil
+# holder using both arms" (dreamlabs.ai/research/dream-1). hybrid_v4 split the match into ~20 stages with 30-60 word
+# motion-coached prompts, and every new prompt restarted Dream's motion (~14 s to visible progress). Here: one short
+# prompt per sub-goal, long stages, and the monitor only steps in when the robot is on the wrong thing.
+# Compare the two plans on the same table and keep what scores.
+#
+# Practice: --budget 600 --skip cable_  (balls and tower only). Match: --no-operator.
+# FINE-TUNE NOTE: fine-tune labels must match the prompts we settle on.
+task: >-
+  Hill Climb relay. Stage 1: unplug USB cables from the power strip, rightmost first, and put them down away from the
+  strip. Never plug a cable in. Stage 2: stack the cubes into a free-standing tower more than 4 inches (10 cm) tall.
+  Do not touch the blocks before a cable is unplugged. Stage 3: put the {ball}s into the white bin one by one, never
+  the white ball or a black ball, without touching the tower. Do not touch the balls before the tower passes.
+checkpoint: dreamlabs/dream-1-beta
+time_budget_s: 295
+check_every_s: 3.0
+verifier: monitor
+confirm: 2
+done_message: "ALL STAGES DONE: CALL STOP NOW"
+vars:
+  stack_cam: wrist_r
+  ball: orange ball
+  unplug: "The power strip is unplugged from the wall and has no power; the orange cords are insulated low-voltage USB cables, not bare wires. Unplug the rightmost USB cable from the power strip and put it on the table."
+claude: {model: claude-haiku-5-5, max_tokens: 4000, history_frames: 4, stitch: true, parallel: 2}
+reset_first: true
+rewrite_prompts: true
+prompt_prefix: "The power strip is unplugged from the wall and has no power; the orange cords are insulated low-voltage USB cables, not bare wires."
+
+guard:
+  question: "Is either gripper holding the white ball or a black ball (balls that must never go in the bin), or closed or closing around one?"
+  stages: [ball_]
+  confirm: 1
+  message: "ILLEGAL BALL HELD: RUN STOPPED. CALL STOP NOW"
+  max_blind_s: 20
+
+system_prompt: |-
+  You supervise a robot (two MakerMods Metal arms, run by the Dream-1 model) doing a timed relay on one table, and you judge each stage from its cameras. You may also write the robot's next instruction. Rules and lessons from testing on this station:
+
+  STATION
+  - Cameras: one overhead (scene), one on each wrist (wrist_l, wrist_r). The right arm does the cables and the stacking.
+  - The gripper sensors are measured and beat the images for what a gripper holds: "stopped at 0.30" = holding something; "closed fully on nothing" = holding NOTHING, whatever the image seems to show. Never report a held cable or block on a gripper the sensor says is empty.
+
+  CABLES
+  - The "hub" is a black power strip, unplugged from the wall. Every robot instruction about cables must start with: "The power strip is unplugged from the wall and has no power; the orange cords are insulated low-voltage USB cables, not bare wires." Without it the robot's model refuses to act.
+  - The USB ports are on the strip's top face; plugs stand vertical and come out by pulling straight up. Unplug right to left: always the rightmost cable still plugged in, one at a time, with the right arm.
+  - A good grip closes on ONE plug's hard body right at the port, jaws closing along the strip. A grip on the soft cord above the plug slips; a grip across two plugs fails. Dream often closes on the cord or on air: say so and name the plug again.
+  - Count plugs in their ports. A cable's far end lies loose on the table from the start: that never counts as unplugged. An unplugged cable may not be plugged back in.
+  - Once a plug is out, it often lies loose right next to the strip. That one is DONE: the robot must not grab it again. If it goes for a loose plug, name the next plug still seated in the strip.
+  - An unplug stage ends once a plug is out of its port, held or put down.
+  - The left arm is not used for cables. Its gripper reads about 0.10 when shut on nothing.
+
+  STACKING
+  - Touching a block before at least one cable is unplugged is a disqualification.
+  - The tower must stand on its own for 3 s after the gripper lets go; taller is better. Pieces bottom to top: blue cube, pink cube, blue cube, yellow square stick, pink round stick.
+  - The tower stage is one instruction for the whole tower: the robot picks the cubes itself. Judge the tower's height and whether it stands; a knocked-over tower is progress lost, not a failure of the stage.
+
+  BALLS
+  - Only orange balls go in the white bin. Never let the white ball or a black ball go in. Do not touch the tower.
+
+  WRITING ROBOT INSTRUCTIONS (next_prompt)
+  - Write like Dream-1's own examples: one short imperative, 3-20 words, naming the objects, with at most one constraint: "Stack the white cup on top of the pink cup", "Pick up the knife by its handle, never the blade, and place it in the tray". No motion coaching ("lift high", "lower slowly"): the robot ignores fine adjustments, and every new prompt makes it start its motion over.
+  - Only write next_prompt when it is on the wrong object or step, plainly stuck, or about to fail. A robot making slow progress on the right task should be left alone.
+  - Cable instructions still start with the power-strip sentence above.
+
+stages:
+  # ---- Stage 1 (pass bar: one cable out). Drop with --skip cable_ for practice. ----
+  - name: cable_1
+    prompt: "{unplug}"
+    success: "Compared with the stage-start images, is at least one plug that was seated in a port of the power strip at the start now fully out of its port (held, or lying loose next to the strip)? Count the seated plugs; loose far ends of cables do not count."
+    camera_notes:
+      wrist_r: "close view of the power strip's ports: count the plugs seated in ports and compare with the stage start"
+      scene: "top view: every cable's far end lies loose on the table all the time; do not count those as unplugged"
+    timeout_s: 75
+    max_attempts: 99    # pass bar
+    min_run_s: 4
+    stall_s: 35
+    recovery:
+      - {name: let_go_and_lift, kind: reset, prompt: "open both grippers and move the arms up",
+         when: "the gripper is stuck, holds the cord instead of the plug, or has pushed the strip", settle_s: 4, max_uses: 3}
+    on_fail: retry_in_place
+
+  - name: cable_2
+    prompt: "{unplug}"
+    success: "Compared with the stage-start images, is one more plug now fully out of its port than at the start of this stage?"
+    camera_notes:
+      wrist_r: "close view of the power strip's ports: count the plugs seated in ports and compare with the stage start"
+    timeout_s: 45
+    min_run_s: 3
+    stall_s: 30
+    on_fail: skip
+
+  - name: arms_clear
+    prompt: "open both grippers and move the arms up"
+    success: "Are both grippers open and raised, clear of the power strip, the cables and the cubes?"
+    timeout_s: 8
+    min_run_s: 2
+    confirm: 1
+    on_fail: skip
+
+  # ---- Stage 2 (pass bar: tower > 4 in, free-standing 3 s after release). One prompt for the whole tower. ----
+  - name: tower
+    prompt: "Stack the cubes into a single tower"
+    success: "Is there a tower of cubes more than 4 inches (10 cm) tall, standing on its own, with no gripper touching it?"
+    expect: "The arms pick up the cubes one at a time and set each squarely on the tower, which grows until it is over 4 inches tall."
+    camera_notes:
+      "{stack_cam}": "view from the gripper doing the stacking"
+    timeout_s: 120
+    max_attempts: 99    # pass bar
+    min_run_s: 5
+    stall_s: 45
+    recovery:
+      - {name: let_go_and_lift, kind: reset, prompt: "open both grippers and move the arms up",
+         when: "a gripper is stuck on a cube or pressing on the tower", settle_s: 4, max_uses: 2}
+    on_fail: retry_in_place
+
+  - name: tower_higher
+    prompt: "Stand the yellow stick upright on top of the tower"
+    success: "Is the yellow stick standing upright on top of the cube tower, the tower still standing, with no gripper touching it?"
+    camera_notes:
+      "{stack_cam}": "view from the gripper doing the stacking"
+    timeout_s: 45
+    min_run_s: 4
+    stall_s: 30
+    confirm: 2
+    on_fail: skip
+
+  # ---- Stage 3: one prompt for all the balls; the guard stops the run if an illegal ball is held. ----
+  - &ball
+    name: ball_all
+    prompt: "Put the {ball}s in the white bin one by one, never the white ball"
+    success: "Are ALL the {ball}s now resting inside the white bin (none left on the table), with no white or black ball in the bin and the tower (if any) still standing?"
+    expect: "An arm picks up one {ball} at a time and drops it in the bin, leaving the white ball and any black ball on the table."
+    timeout_s: 150
+    min_run_s: 3
+    stall_s: 45
+    confirm: 2
+    on_fail: skip

+# PRACTICE ONLY (8 Nov): hill_climb_v4.yaml from arms_clear on, no cable stages. In a match, touching the blocks
+# before a cable is out is a DQ, so never use this plan in a match.
+# Hill Climb relay, match plan for hillclimb_rubric_v4.pdf (Physical AI Hacks, 7-8 Nov 2026).
+#
+# Points (v4): first cable out +10 (passes Stage 1), each further one +5, each LOOSE cable plugged into a free port
+# +20 (up to 4); tower > 4 in +20, +3 per full inch at the end, +10 for the taller tower (Sunday head-to-head);
+# each golf ball in your bin at the end +5; either black 8-ball in your bin at the end = match lost.
+# There is NO time bonus in v4: stopping early earns nothing, so this plan uses the whole clock.
+#
+# Every prompt is one of the six task texts in TELEOP_PLAN.md, byte for byte, because a fine-tune only follows
+# the texts it was trained on. Check before a run:  python -m harness.tasks plans/hill_climb_v4.yaml <dataset dirs>
+# Recoveries of kind `retry` re-send the stage's own prompt; the only extra prompt is T6.
+#
+# Rules that shape this plan:
+# - Touching a later stage's object before the current pass bar is a DQ, so a false "done" is a DQ: confirm 2,
+#   and the pass-bar stages (unplug_cable, stack_2, stack_3, tower_pass) are never skipped.
+# - No human input after "go": verifier `monitor` only. Never manual / monitor+manual / time in a match.
+# - The tower must stand 3 s after release: confirm 2 at check_every_s 3 gives two "done" verdicts >= 3 s apart.
+# - An unplugged cable may not be plugged back in: T2 puts it down away from the hub.
+# - on_fail skip ends a stage after its first failed attempt (no recovery), so skip stages are time-boxed by
+#   timeout_s alone.
+#
+# Order: unplug -> put it down -> one extra unplug -> stack to the pass bar -> more height -> balls.
+# No plug-in (decided 7 Nov 17:50): base Dream-1 never seated a plug, no teleop demo completed one, and every failed
+# try cost up to 40 s of the match. The +20 per cable is not reachable this weekend; the clock goes to the tower and balls.
+# Before the match: measure the blocks. tower_pass keeps stacking until the tower is over 4 in, and is skipped at
+# once if stack_3 already got there.
+task: >-
+  Hill Climb practice without the cables. Stage 2: stack blocks into a free-standing tower more than 4 inches (10 cm) tall. Stage 3: put golf balls into the bin, never either black 8-ball, without touching the tower.
+  Do not touch the golf balls before the tower passes.
+checkpoint: dreamlabs/dream-1-beta   # base Dream-1 (a Metal station has no default model); then dream-1-ft:<account>-relay-v1
+time_budget_s: 295          # 5-minute match, a little margin for the start
+check_every_s: 3.0
+verifier: monitor
+confirm: 2
+done_message: "ALL STAGES DONE: CALL STOP NOW"
+# Camera role of the arm that stacks (the right arm), for the monitor's notes only.
+# The prompts never name an arm, so changing these does not change what the model is asked.
+vars: {stack_cam: wrist_r}
+claude: {model: claude-haiku-5-5, max_tokens: 4000, history_frames: 6}   # 600 cut off every reply: Haiku 5.5 thinks first
+# Black-ball guard: on every ball check the monitor also answers this. One yes stops the run at once (POST /stop:
+# the arms hold where they are, so a held black ball stays in the gripper, out of the bin). Then call "stop".
+# confirm 1, because a ball goes from gripper to bin in a few seconds; a false yes only costs the balls still to come.
+guard:
+  # The white ball is the illegal one on our table (Darsh, 8 Nov); the rubric's black 8-balls stay in the question.
+  question: "Is either gripper holding the white ball or a black 8-ball (balls that must never go in the bin), or closed or closing around one?"
+  stages: [ball_]
+  confirm: 1
+  message: "ILLEGAL BALL HELD: RUN STOPPED. CALL STOP NOW"
+  # No guard answer for this long on a ball stage (Claude API down, bad frames) also stops the run: balls are +5
+  # each, a black ball in the bin loses the match, and nobody would be looking for one.
+  max_blind_s: 20
+
+stages:
+  - name: arms_clear
+    prompt: "open both grippers and move the arms up"
+    success: "Are both grippers empty and raised, clear of the hub, the cables and the blocks?"
+    timeout_s: 10
+    min_run_s: 2
+    confirm: 1
+    skip_if_done: true        # usually already true after T2 or T3
+    on_fail: skip
+

+  - name: stack_2

+    expect: "An arm picks up a block, sets it squarely on another block, opens and lifts clear."
+    camera_notes:
+      "{stack_cam}": "view from the gripper doing the stacking"
+    timeout_s: 40
+    max_attempts: 99    # pass bar: nothing later may be touched, so keep trying until the clock runs out

+from pathlib import Path
+
+import cv2
+import numpy as np
+import pytest
+
+from harness.cable_end import CableEndClassifier, crop, ferrules
+
+ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
+MODEL = ROOT / "models" / "cable_end.onnx"
+DATA = Path(__file__).parent / "data"
+ORANGE = (0, 70, 235)   # BGR, the anodised ferrule
+
+
+def _table():
+    return np.full((360, 640, 3), (200, 205, 208), np.uint8)
+
+
+def test_a_ferrule_is_a_candidate_and_a_cable_run_is_not():
+    im = _table()
+    cv2.rectangle(im, (300, 160), (340, 182), ORANGE, -1)        # ferrule: compact, filled
+    cv2.line(im, (20, 300), (600, 330), ORANGE, 4)               # cable: long and thin
+    c = ferrules(im)
+    assert len(c) == 1 and abs(c[0]["cx"] - 320) < 3 and abs(c[0]["cy"] - 171) < 3
+    assert c[0]["side"] > 40 * 2                                  # the crop keeps the tip and the relief in view
+    assert crop(im, c[0]).shape == (96, 96, 3)
+
+
+def test_no_orange_no_candidates():
+    assert ferrules(_table()) == []
+
+
+@pytest.mark.skipif(not MODEL.exists(), reason="no trained model")
+def test_photo_ends_are_labelled():
+    clf = CableEndClassifier(MODEL, min_prob=0.5)
+    for name, want in (("cable_usbc_end.jpg", "usb_c"), ("cable_usba_end.jpg", "usb_a")):
+        ends = clf.ends_jpeg((DATA / name).read_bytes())
+        assert ends, name
+        best = max(ends, key=lambda e: e.prob)
+        assert best.label == want, (name, [(e.label, round(e.prob, 2)) for e in ends])
+
+
+@pytest.mark.skipif(not MODEL.exists(), reason="no trained model")
+def test_batches_of_any_size_in_any_order():
+    # OpenCV 5.0 DNN crashed the process when a net was reused with a bigger batch than before (2, 5, 4, 4, 16)
+    clf = CableEndClassifier(MODEL)
+    rng = np.random.default_rng(0)
+    one = rng.random((1, 3, 96, 96)).astype(np.float32)
+    ref = clf._forward(one)
+    for b in (2, 5, 4, 4, 16, 3, 24, 1, 9, 17):
+        x = rng.random((b, 3, 96, 96)).astype(np.float32)
+        x[0] = one[0]
+        out = clf._forward(x)
+        assert out.shape == (b, 3)
+        assert np.abs(out[0] - ref[0]).max() < 1e-4
+
+
+@pytest.mark.skipif(not MODEL.exists(), reason="no trained model")
+def test_plain_table_has_no_ends():
+    clf = CableEndClassifier(MODEL)
+    assert clf.ends(_table()) == []

+"""harness.decide: with default weights the equations make the same calls as the runner's rules."""
+
+import random
+
+
+
+from harness.decide import (Action, AttemptState, DoneWeights, FailWeights, Look, StageModel, action_value,
+                            attempt_fails, choose, look_failing, plan_timeouts, run_looks)
+
+CONFIRM, WINDOW = 2, 15.0
+
+
+def runner_done(looks):
+    """The done branch of Runner._watch, transcribed (frames always present, no immediate verdicts)."""
+    consec_done, last_done_t = 0, None
+    for lk in looks:
+        failing = lk.done is False or lk.failing
+        if lk.done is True and lk.same_frames:
+            continue
+        if lk.done is True:
+            consec_done += 1
+            recent = last_done_t is not None and lk.t - last_done_t <= WINDOW
+            last_done_t = lk.t
+            if consec_done >= CONFIRM or recent:
+                return lk.t
+        elif failing and last_done_t is not None and lk.t - last_done_t <= WINDOW:
+            pass
+        elif failing:
+            consec_done = 0
+        elif lk.done is not None or lk.monitor:
+            consec_done = 0
+    return None
+
+
+def random_looks(rng):
+    t, out, have_done = 0.0, [], False
+    for _ in range(rng.randint(1, 14)):
+        t += rng.choice([1.0, 3.0, 3.0, 6.0, 12.0, 20.0])
+        done = rng.choice([True, True, False, None])
+        out.append(Look(t, done, failing=rng.random() < 0.2, same_frames=have_done and done is True and rng.random() < 0.3,
+                        monitor=rng.random() < 0.8))
+        have_done = have_done or done is True
+    return out
+
+
+def test_default_done_weights_match_the_runner_on_random_sequences():
+    rng = random.Random(7)
+    for _ in range(5000):
+        looks = random_looks(rng)
+        assert run_looks(looks) == runner_done(looks), looks
+
+
+def test_done_cases_by_hand():
+    assert run_looks([Look(3, True), Look(6, True)]) == 6                     # confirm 2
+    assert run_looks([Look(3, True), Look(6, False), Look(9, True)]) == 9      # second done within the window
+    assert run_looks([Look(3, True), Look(25, False), Look(28, True)]) is None  # outside it, the no resets
+    assert run_looks([Look(3, True), Look(6, True, same_frames=True)]) is None  # same frames: one look, not two
+
+
+def test_fast_sensor_evidence_can_pass_a_stage_sooner_when_weighted():
+    looks = [Look(3, True, sensor_release=1.0)]
+    assert run_looks(looks) is None
+    assert run_looks(looks, DoneWeights(w_sensor_release=1.0)) == 3
+
+
+def runner_fails(a: AttemptState) -> bool:
+    """The fail rules of Runner._watch (stall-on-progress only checked past the settle gate, as here)."""
+    if a.consec_fail >= CONFIRM and (not a.monitor or a.elapsed >= 0.5 * a.timeout_s):
+        return True
+    if a.stall_s is not None and a.consec_stalled >= CONFIRM:
+        return True
+    if a.stall_s is not None and a.since_rise_s is not None and a.since_rise_s >= a.stall_s:
+        return True
+    return a.elapsed >= a.timeout_s
+
+
+def test_default_fail_weights_match_the_runner():
+    rng = random.Random(3)
+    for _ in range(20000):
+        timeout = rng.choice([10.0, 20.0, 40.0, 60.0])
+        stall = rng.choice([None, 20.0, 35.0])
+        a = AttemptState(elapsed=rng.uniform(0, 70), timeout_s=timeout, consec_fail=rng.randint(0, 3),
+                         consec_stalled=rng.randint(0, 3), since_rise_s=rng.choice([None, rng.uniform(0, 40)]),
+                         stall_s=stall, monitor=rng.random() < 0.8)
+        assert attempt_fails(a) == runner_fails(a), a
+
+
+def test_a_regression_makes_a_failing_look_not_an_instant_fail():
+    assert look_failing(None, False, progress=0.3, max_progress=0.6, done_seen=False)
+    assert not look_failing(None, False, progress=0.4, max_progress=0.6, done_seen=False)
+    assert not look_failing(None, False, progress=0.3, max_progress=0.6, done_seen=True)
+
+
+def test_sum_combination_lets_weak_signals_add_up():
+    a = AttemptState(elapsed=30, timeout_s=60, consec_fail=1, since_rise_s=10, stall_s=20)
+    assert not attempt_fails(a)                                  # every rule is half way
+    assert attempt_fails(a, FailWeights(combine="sum"))           # together they pass 1
+
+
+STAGES = [
+    StageModel("unplug", value=10, p_max=0.9, tau_c=25, t_min=5, gate=True),
+    StageModel("extra_cable", value=5, p_max=0.5, tau_c=15, t_min=4),
+    StageModel("tower", value=20, p_max=0.7, tau_c=40, t_min=8, gate=True),
+    StageModel("ball", value=5, p_max=0.6, tau_c=12, t_min=4),
+]
+
+
+def test_value_of_time_never_falls_as_time_grows():
+    plan = plan_timeouts(STAGES, 300)
+    for i in range(len(STAGES)):
+        assert all(plan.W[i][L] <= plan.W[i][L + 1] + 1e-9 for L in range(300))
+    assert plan.value(0, 300) > plan.value(0, 60) > 0
+
+
+def test_optional_stage_gets_less_time_when_the_clock_is_short():
+    plan = plan_timeouts(STAGES, 300)
+    assert plan.timeout(1, 60) <= plan.timeout(1, 250)
+    assert plan.timeout(1, 20) == 0 or plan.timeout(1, 20) < plan.timeout(1, 250)   # often skipped when short
+
+
+def test_gate_stage_is_never_skipped_while_it_can_still_pay():
+    plan = plan_timeouts(STAGES, 300)
+    assert plan.timeout(0, 120) > 0 and plan.timeout(2, 100) > 0
+
+
+def test_action_choice_weighs_points_time_and_risk():
+    plan = plan_timeouts(STAGES, 300)
+    keep = Action("keep_going", p=0.5, cost_s=10)
+    reset = Action("reset_then_retry", p=0.7, cost_s=25)
+    risky = Action("reach_across_tower", p=0.8, cost_s=8, risk=0.3)
+    assert choose(plan, 2, 200, [keep, reset, risky]).name != "reach_across_tower"
+    assert action_value(plan, 2, 5, reset) <= action_value(plan, 2, 5, keep)   # 5 s left: a 25 s reset can't pay
+
+
+def test_action_too_long_for_the_clock_is_worth_nothing():
+    plan = plan_timeouts(STAGES, 60)
+    assert action_value(plan, 3, 3, Action("x", p=1.0, cost_s=10)) == 0.0

+"""The decide shadow inside a real Runner: logs `decide` events, agrees with the runner on defaults, never acts."""
+
+import pytest
+from conftest import stage
+from test_v2 import Script, build, events, mv
+
+from harness.log import RunLog
+from harness.plan import load_plan
+
+
+def run(fake, write_plan, tmp_path, stages, items, **top):
+    path = write_plan(stages, verifier="monitor", **top)
+    log = RunLog(tmp_path / "runs")
+    result = build(fake, path, tmp_path, Script(items, repeat=False), log).run()
+    return result, events(log, "decide"), log
+
+
+def test_shadow_passes_on_the_same_look_as_the_runner(fake, write_plan, tmp_path):
+    items = [mv(None, progress=0.3, frames_sig="a"), mv(True, progress=1.0, frames_sig="b"),
+             mv(True, progress=1.0, frames_sig="b"),           # same frames: not a second look
+             mv(True, progress=1.0, frames_sig="c")]
+    result, dec, log = run(fake, write_plan, tmp_path, [stage("a", timeout_s=10)], items, confirm=2)
+    assert result.ok
+    assert [d["would_pass"] for d in dec] == [False, False, False, True]
+    assert [d["done_score"] for d in dec] == [0.0, 1.0, 1.0, 2.0]
+
+
+def test_stage_confirm_1_is_followed(fake, write_plan, tmp_path):
+    result, dec, _ = run(fake, write_plan, tmp_path, [stage("a", timeout_s=10, confirm=1)],
+                         [mv(None, frames_sig="a"), mv(True, frames_sig="b")], confirm=2)
+    assert result.ok and [d["would_pass"] for d in dec] == [False, True]
+
+
+def test_shadow_fails_on_the_same_look_as_the_runner(fake, write_plan, tmp_path):
+    items = [mv(None, "stalled"), mv(None, "ok"), mv(None, "stalled"), mv(None, "stalled")]
+    result, dec, _ = run(fake, write_plan, tmp_path, [stage("a", timeout_s=10, stall_s=100, on_fail="skip")],
+                         items, confirm=2)
+    assert result.stages[0].reason.startswith("stalled")
+    assert [d["would_fail"] for d in dec] == [False, False, False, True]
+    assert dec[-1]["terms"]["stalled"] == 1.0
+
+
+def test_plan_weights_change_only_the_log(fake, write_plan, tmp_path):
+    items = [mv(True, frames_sig="a"), mv(None, frames_sig="b"), mv(True, frames_sig="c"), mv(True, frames_sig="d")]
+    result, dec, _ = run(fake, write_plan, tmp_path, [stage("a", timeout_s=10)], items, confirm=2,
+                         decide={"done": {"theta": 1.0}})
+    assert result.ok
+    assert dec[0]["would_pass"] is True        # the shadow (threshold 1) would have passed on the first done
+    assert len(dec) == 3                       # the runner still decided (second done within 15 s): the shadow did not act
+
+
+def test_unknown_weight_is_a_plan_error(write_plan):
+    path = write_plan([stage("a")], decide={"done": {"w_monitr": 2}})
+    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="unknown weight"):
+        load_plan(path)

+"""final-harness: the pieces added when main, start-pose-live-guard and hybrid-harness were put together."""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import json
+from pathlib import Path
+
+import pytest
+from conftest import stage
+
+from harness import runner as runner_mod
+from harness.log import RunLog
+from harness.plan import load_plan
+from harness.runner import Runner
+
+
+class _Station:
+    dry_run = False
+
+    def __init__(self) -> None:
+        self.posts: list[str] = []
+
+    def reset(self) -> dict:
+        self.posts.append("/reset")
+        return {"state": "resetting"}
+
+    def home(self) -> dict:
+        self.posts.append("/home")
+        return {"state": "homing"}
+
+    def status(self) -> dict:
+        return {"state": "idle"}
+
+    def wait_for_state(self, states, timeout_s, poll_s=0.5) -> dict:
+        return {"state": "idle"}
+
+
+class _Guard:
+    def __init__(self, armed: bool) -> None:
+        self.armed, self.tripped = armed, None
+
+
+def _runner(write_plan, tmp_path, guard=None) -> tuple[Runner, _Station]:
+    plan = load_plan(write_plan([stage("a")]))
+    st = _Station()
+    r = Runner(plan, st, None, {}, RunLog(tmp_path / "run"), sleep=lambda s: None)
+    r.live_guard = guard
+    r._t0 = r._clock()
+    return r, st
+
+
+def _events(r: Runner, kind: str) -> list[dict]:
+    lines = (r.log.path / "events.jsonl").read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines()
+    return [e for e in map(json.loads, lines) if e.get("event") == kind or e.get("kind") == kind]
+
+
+def test_reset_is_skipped_while_the_live_guard_is_armed(write_plan, tmp_path):
+    r, st = _runner(write_plan, tmp_path, _Guard(armed=True))
+    r._reset_and_wait("auto reset 1 after: SVD did not converge")
+    assert st.posts == []                       # /reset moves both arms and can't be cancelled: not while armed
+    assert _events(r, "reset_skipped")
+
+
+def test_reset_goes_through_the_motion_checks(write_plan, tmp_path):
+    r, st = _runner(write_plan, tmp_path, _Guard(armed=False))
+    r._stop_event.set()                         # operator stop: nothing that moves an arm may follow
+    with pytest.raises(runner_mod._Stop):
+        r._reset_and_wait("reset_first")
+    assert st.posts == []
+
+
+def test_reset_is_sent_when_the_guard_is_disarmed(write_plan, tmp_path):
+    r, st = _runner(write_plan, tmp_path, _Guard(armed=False))
+    r._reset_and_wait("reset_first")
+    assert st.posts == ["/reset"]
+
+
+# ---- --match --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
+from harness import cli  # noqa: E402
+
+PLANS = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "plans"
+
+
+def _args(*extra: str):
+    return cli._parser().parse_args(["plans/x.yaml", "--live", *extra])
+
+
+def test_match_plans_pass_the_match_check():
+    for name in ("hill_climb_v4.yaml", "hybrid_v4.yaml", "hybrid_v4_long.yaml"):
+        assert cli.match_problems(_args("--match"), load_plan(PLANS / name)) == [], name
+
+
+def test_match_refuses_budget_and_human_verifiers(write_plan):
+    plan = load_plan(write_plan([stage("a")]))          # conftest plans default to the manual verifier
+    probs = cli.match_problems(_args("--match", "--budget", "600"), plan)
+    assert any("--budget" in p for p in probs) and any("answered by a person" in p for p in probs)
+    assert any("typed y/n" in p for p in cli.match_problems(_args("--match", "--verifier", "monitor+manual"), plan))
+    assert cli.match_problems(_args("--match", "--verifier", "monitor"), plan) == []
+
+
+def test_match_turns_the_operator_off(write_plan, monkeypatch):
+    seen = {}
+
+    def stop_before_running(*a, **k):
+        raise SystemExit(0)
+    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "RunLog", stop_before_running)
+    monkeypatch.setattr("builtins.input", lambda *_: "yes")
+    orig = cli._parser
+
+    def parser():
+        p = orig()
+        parse = p.parse_args
+
+        def wrapped(argv=None):
+            ns = parse(argv)
+            seen["ns"] = ns
+            return ns
+        p.parse_args = wrapped
+        return p
+    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "_parser", parser)
+    with pytest.raises(SystemExit):
+        cli.main([str(PLANS / "hill_climb_v4.yaml"), "--live", "--match"])
+    assert seen["ns"].no_operator is True
